## Summary: Transcript Handling Across External Agents

Based on my thorough exploration of the external-agents repository, here's what I found about how agents handle transcripts:

### Repository Structure
- **Only one agent implementation exists**: `agents/entire-agent-kiro/` (Kiro agent)
- No other agent implementations are present in the `/agents/` directory
- The test infrastructure in `/e2e/` is shared across all agents via subpackages (`e2e/agents/`, `e2e/kiro/`, etc.)

### Kiro Agent: Transcript Handling Flow

#### 1. **Transcript Capture at Stop (`captureTranscriptForStop`)**
Location: `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go:178-186`

```go
func (a *Agent) captureTranscriptForStop(cwd string, sessionID string) string {
	if sessionRef, err := a.ensureCachedTranscript(cwd, sessionID); err == nil && sessionRef != "" {
		return sessionRef
	}
	if sessionRef, err := a.ensureIDETranscript(cwd, sessionID); err == nil && sessionRef != "" {
		return sessionRef
	}
	return a.createPlaceholderTranscript(cwd, sessionID)
}
```

**Key behavior**: Attempts to capture the transcript from three sources in order:
1. Kiro CLI SQLite database (`.entire/tmp/<sessionID>.json`)
2. Kiro IDE workspace sessions (converts IDE format to CLI format)
3. Placeholder empty JSON object `{}`

**Important**: The captured transcript is the **complete, cumulative session history** - no trimming or filtering happens at capture time.

#### 2. **ReadSession Returns Full Native Data**
Location: `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/agent.go:54-88`

```go
func (a *Agent) ReadSession(input *protocol.HookInputJSON) (protocol.AgentSessionJSON, error) {
	// ... session setup ...
	var nativeData []byte
	if sessionRef != "" {
		data, err := os.ReadFile(sessionRef)
		// ... error handling ...
		nativeData = data
	}
	return protocol.AgentSessionJSON{
		SessionID:     sessionID,
		NativeData:    nativeData,  // FULL transcript bytes
		// ... other fields ...
	}, nil
}
```

**Key behavior**: Reads the entire session file and returns it as `NativeData` without modification.

#### 3. **ReadTranscript Returns Raw Bytes**
Location: `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go:33-35`

```go
func (a *Agent) ReadTranscript(sessionRef string) ([]byte, error) {
	return os.ReadFile(sessionRef)
}
```

**Key behavior**: Simple passthrough - reads and returns the entire file as-is, no processing or filtering.

#### 4. **Transcript Analysis Uses Offsets (NOT Delta)**
Location: `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go:265-280`

```go
func (a *Agent) ExtractModifiedFiles(path string, offset int) ([]string, int, error) {
	transcript, err := readAndParseTranscript(path)
	// ...
	totalEntries := len(transcript.History)
	if offset >= totalEntries {
		return nil, totalEntries, nil
	}
	// Returns files from History[offset:], NOT just the delta since last call
	return extractModifiedFilesFromHistory(transcript.History[offset:]), totalEntries, nil
}
```

**Key behavior**: 
- `offset` parameter points to the starting index in the history
- Returns all entries **from offset to end** - NOT just the delta
- Also returns `currentPosition` (total history length)
- The Entire CLI uses the offset to extract incremental data (delta) from the **full transcript**

#### 5. **Checkpoints Store Full Cumulative Transcript**

From test artifacts (`/e2e/artifacts/*/checkpoint-metadata/*/0/metadata.json`):

```json
{
  "transcript_path": "/9f/14d3592033/0/full.jsonl"
}
```

The checkpoint stores `full.jsonl` - indicating the **full, complete transcript**, not a delta.

### How Checkpoints Work with Transcripts

From the test validation code (`e2e/testutil/assertions.go:303-314`):

```go
// Validate transcript is valid JSONL
transcriptBlob := fmt.Sprintf("entire/checkpoints/v1:%s/0/full.jsonl", path)
transcriptRaw := gitOutputSafe(dir, "show", transcriptBlob)
// ... validates full transcript exists and contains expected content
```

**Key insight**: 
- The checkpoint branch (`entire/checkpoints/v1`) stores the **full cumulative transcript**
- File naming (`full.jsonl`) explicitly indicates it's the complete history
- No trimming or filtering happens before checkpoint storage
- The transcript grows monotonically with each session

### Session Metadata Storage

From actual checkpoint metadata (line 59 in `e2e/testutil/metadata.go`):

```go
type SessionMetadata struct {
	TranscriptPath string `json:"transcript_path"`
	// ... other fields ...
}
```

Each session's metadata points to its transcript path, which contains the **full history up to that session's end**.

### Offset/Position Pattern

The protocol defines these methods with offset support:

```go
// From protocol/protocol.go
ExtractModifiedFiles(path string, offset int) ([]string, int, error)
ExtractPrompts(sessionRef string, offset int) ([]string, error)
GetTranscriptPosition(path string) (int, error)
```

**Pattern**: 
- `GetTranscriptPosition` returns the **total history length** (number of entries)
- `ExtractModifiedFiles` returns entries from `[offset:]`, allowing incremental extraction
- The Entire CLI uses positions to determine what's "new" since the last checkpoint
- But the **stored transcript is always complete**, not delta-only

### No Trimming or Filtering

Across all code paths:
1. **`captureTranscriptForStop`**: Copies entire native session file, no filtering
2. **`ReadSession`**: Returns `NativeData` as complete file bytes
3. **`ReadTranscript`**: Passthrough read of full file
4. **Checkpoints**: Store as `full.jsonl` (cumulative history)
5. **Transcript Analysis**: Uses offset to read from full history, not deltas

### Key Finding: Entire CLI Handles Deltas, Not Agents

The agent returns the **full cumulative transcript**. The Entire CLI (external process) is responsible for:
1. Getting the transcript position from the last checkpoint
2. Calling `ExtractModifiedFiles(offset=lastPosition)` to get NEW changes
3. Storing the full transcript in the checkpoint

The offset/position pattern enables **incremental extraction** from the full transcript, allowing the Entire CLI to determine what changed since the last checkpoint without the agent needing to maintain delta state.

### Test Evidence

From `e2e/lifecycle_test.go`:
- `TestLifecycle_MultiplePromptsManualCommit`: Creates two files with two prompts, commits once, verifies checkpoint advances (covering **both prompts**)
- `TestLifecycle_RewindAfterCommit`: Agent commits file, condensation occurs, shadow branches become logs-only
- The checkpoint metadata shows `files_touched: ["alpha.txt"]` for a single prompt - the checkpoint captures the **delta of work for that turn/session**, extracted from the **full cumulative transcript**

This architecture separates concerns:
- **Agents**: Maintain and return complete session state
- **Entire CLI**: Extracts deltas using offset/position metadata, stores as checkpoints

     1→package testutil

     5→// TokenUsage tracks API token consumption for a checkpoint.
     6→type TokenUsage struct {
     7→	InputTokens         int `json:"input_tokens"`
     8→	CacheCreationTokens int `json:"cache_creation_tokens"`
     9→	CacheReadTokens     int `json:"cache_read_tokens"`
    10→	OutputTokens        int `json:"output_tokens"`
    11→	APICallCount        int `json:"api_call_count"`
    12→}
    13→
    14→// Attribution tracks code authorship for a checkpoint.
    15→type Attribution struct {
    16→	CalculatedAt    time.Time `json:"calculated_at"`
    17→	AgentLines      int       `json:"agent_lines"`
    18→	HumanAdded      int       `json:"human_added"`
    19→	HumanModified   int       `json:"human_modified"`
    20→	HumanRemoved    int       `json:"human_removed"`
    21→	TotalCommitted  int       `json:"total_committed"`
    22→	AgentPercentage float64   `json:"agent_percentage"`
    23→}
    24→
    25→// CheckpointMetadata represents the top-level metadata.json for a checkpoint.
    26→type CheckpointMetadata struct {
    27→	CLIVersion       string       `json:"cli_version"`
    28→	CheckpointID     string       `json:"checkpoint_id"`
    29→	Strategy         string       `json:"strategy"`
    30→	Branch           string       `json:"branch"`
    31→	CheckpointsCount int          `json:"checkpoints_count"`
    32→	FilesTouched     []string     `json:"files_touched"`
    33→	Sessions         []SessionRef `json:"sessions"`
    34→	TokenUsage       TokenUsage   `json:"token_usage"`
    35→}
    36→
    37→// SessionRef is a pointer to a session within checkpoint metadata.
    38→type SessionRef struct {
    39→	Metadata    string `json:"metadata"`
    40→	Transcript  string `json:"transcript"`
    41→	Context     string `json:"context"`
    42→	ContentHash string `json:"content_hash"`
    43→	Prompt      string `json:"prompt"`
    44→}
    45→
    46→// SessionMetadata represents a session's metadata.json within a checkpoint.
    47→type SessionMetadata struct {
    48→	CLIVersion         string      `json:"cli_version"`
    49→	CheckpointID       string      `json:"checkpoint_id"`
    50→	SessionID          string      `json:"session_id"`
    51→	Strategy           string      `json:"strategy"`
    52→	CreatedAt          time.Time   `json:"created_at"`
    53→	Branch             string      `json:"branch"`
    54→	Agent              string      `json:"agent"`
    55→	CheckpointsCount   int         `json:"checkpoints_count"`
    56→	FilesTouched       []string    `json:"files_touched"`
    57→	TokenUsage         TokenUsage  `json:"token_usage"`
    58→	InitialAttribution Attribution `json:"initial_attribution"`
    59→	TranscriptPath     string      `json:"transcript_path"`
    60→}
    61→
    62→// CheckpointPath converts a checkpoint ID to its two-level directory path.
    63→func CheckpointPath(id string) string {
    64→	return id[:2] + "/" + id[2:]
    65→}
    66→

    19→// DeepCheckpointValidation contains expected values for comprehensive checkpoint validation.

    28→var hexIDPattern = regexp.MustCompile(`^[0-9a-f]{12}$`)
    29→
    30→// AssertFileExists asserts that at least one file matches the glob pattern
    31→// relative to dir.
    32→func AssertFileExists(t *testing.T, dir string, glob string) {
    33→	t.Helper()
    34→	matches, err := filepath.Glob(filepath.Join(dir, glob))
    35→	require.NoError(t, err)
    36→	assert.NotEmpty(t, matches, "expected files matching %s in %s", glob, dir)
    37→}
    38→
    39→// WaitForFileExists polls until at least one file matches the glob pattern
    40→// relative to dir, or fails the test after timeout.
    41→func WaitForFileExists(t *testing.T, dir string, glob string, timeout time.Duration) {
    42→	t.Helper()
    43→	pattern := filepath.Join(dir, glob)
    44→	deadline := time.Now().Add(timeout)
    45→	for {
    46→		matches, err := filepath.Glob(pattern)
    47→		require.NoError(t, err)
    48→		if len(matches) > 0 {
    49→			return
    50→		}
    51→		if time.Now().After(deadline) {
    52→			t.Fatalf("expected files matching %s in %s within %s", glob, dir, timeout)
    53→		}
    54→		time.Sleep(500 * time.Millisecond)
    55→	}
    56→}
    57→
    58→// AssertNewCommits polls until at least `atLeast` new commits exist since setup,
    59→// or fails after 20 seconds.
    60→func AssertNewCommits(t *testing.T, s *RepoState, atLeast int) {
    61→	t.Helper()
    62→	deadline := time.Now().Add(20 * time.Second)
    63→	for {
    64→		out := GitOutput(t, s.Dir, "log", "--oneline", s.HeadBefore+"..HEAD")
    65→		var lines []string
    66→		if out != "" {
    67→			lines = strings.Split(strings.TrimSpace(out), "\n")
    68→		}
    69→		if len(lines) >= atLeast {
    70→			return
    71→		}
    72→		if time.Now().After(deadline) {
    73→			t.Fatalf("expected at least %d new commit(s), got %d after 20s", atLeast, len(lines))
    74→		}
    75→		time.Sleep(500 * time.Millisecond)
    76→	}
    77→}
    78→
    79→// WaitForCheckpoint polls until the checkpoint branch advances from its
    80→// initial state, or fails the test after timeout.
    81→func WaitForCheckpoint(t *testing.T, s *RepoState, timeout time.Duration) {
    82→	t.Helper()
    83→	deadline := time.Now().Add(timeout)
    84→	for time.Now().Before(deadline) {
    85→		after := GitOutput(t, s.Dir, "rev-parse", "entire/checkpoints/v1")
    86→		if after != s.CheckpointBefore {
    87→			return
    88→		}
    89→		time.Sleep(200 * time.Millisecond)
    90→	}
    91→	t.Fatalf("checkpoint branch did not advance within %s", timeout)
    92→}
    93→
    94→// WaitForCheckpointAdvanceFrom polls until the checkpoint branch advances from
    95→// the given ref, or fails the test after timeout.
    96→func WaitForCheckpointAdvanceFrom(t *testing.T, dir string, fromRef string, timeout time.Duration) {
    97→	t.Helper()
    98→	deadline := time.Now().Add(timeout)
    99→	for time.Now().Before(deadline) {
   100→		after := GitOutput(t, dir, "rev-parse", "entire/checkpoints/v1")
   101→		if after != fromRef {
   102→			return
   103→		}
   104→		time.Sleep(200 * time.Millisecond)
   105→	}
   106→	t.Fatalf("checkpoint branch did not advance from %s within %s", fromRef[:8], timeout)
   107→}
   108→
   109→// AssertCheckpointAdvanced asserts the checkpoint branch moved forward.
   110→func AssertCheckpointAdvanced(t *testing.T, s *RepoState) {
   111→	t.Helper()
   112→	after := GitOutput(t, s.Dir, "rev-parse", "entire/checkpoints/v1")
   113→	assert.NotEqual(t, s.CheckpointBefore, after, "checkpoint branch did not advance")
   114→}
   115→
   116→// AssertCheckpointNotAdvanced asserts the checkpoint branch has NOT moved.
   117→func AssertCheckpointNotAdvanced(t *testing.T, s *RepoState) {
   118→	t.Helper()
   119→	after := GitOutput(t, s.Dir, "rev-parse", "entire/checkpoints/v1")
   120→	assert.Equal(t, s.CheckpointBefore, after, "checkpoint branch advanced unexpectedly")
   121→}
   122→
   123→// AssertCheckpointIDFormat asserts the checkpoint ID is 12 lowercase hex chars.
   124→func AssertCheckpointIDFormat(t *testing.T, checkpointID string) {
   125→	t.Helper()
   126→	assert.Regexp(t, hexIDPattern, checkpointID,
   127→		"checkpoint ID %q should be 12 lowercase hex chars", checkpointID)
   128→}
   129→
   130→// AssertHasCheckpointTrailer asserts the commit has an Entire-Checkpoint trailer,
   131→// validates its format, and returns its value.
   132→func AssertHasCheckpointTrailer(t *testing.T, dir string, ref string) string {
   133→	t.Helper()
   134→	trailer := GetCheckpointTrailer(t, dir, ref)
   135→	require.NotEmpty(t, trailer, "no Entire-Checkpoint trailer on %s", ref)
   136→	AssertCheckpointIDFormat(t, trailer)
   137→	return trailer
   138→}
   139→
   140→// AssertNoCheckpointTrailer asserts the commit does NOT have an Entire-Checkpoint trailer.
   141→func AssertNoCheckpointTrailer(t *testing.T, dir string, ref string) {
   142→	t.Helper()
   143→	trailer := GetCheckpointTrailer(t, dir, ref)
   144→	assert.Empty(t, trailer, "expected no Entire-Checkpoint trailer on %s, got %q", ref, trailer)
   145→}
   146→
   147→// AssertCheckpointExists asserts that the checkpoint ID is mentioned on
   148→// the checkpoint branch and that its metadata.json exists in the tree.
   149→func AssertCheckpointExists(t *testing.T, dir string, checkpointID string) {
   150→	t.Helper()
   151→	out := GitOutput(t, dir, "log", "entire/checkpoints/v1", "--grep="+checkpointID, "--oneline")
   152→	assert.NotEmpty(t, out, "checkpoint %s not found on checkpoint branch", checkpointID)
   153→
   154→	path := CheckpointPath(checkpointID) + "/metadata.json"
   155→	blob := "entire/checkpoints/v1:" + path
   156→	raw := gitOutputSafe(dir, "show", blob)
   157→	assert.NotEmpty(t, raw,
   158→		"checkpoint %s metadata not found at %s", checkpointID, path)
   159→}
   160→
   161→// AssertCommitLinkedToCheckpoint asserts the trailer exists AND the
   162→// checkpoint data exists on the checkpoint branch.
   163→func AssertCommitLinkedToCheckpoint(t *testing.T, dir string, ref string) {
   164→	t.Helper()
   165→	trailer := AssertHasCheckpointTrailer(t, dir, ref)
   166→	AssertCheckpointExists(t, dir, trailer)
   167→}
   168→
   169→// AssertCheckpointHasSingleSession asserts checkpoint metadata has exactly one session.
   170→func AssertCheckpointHasSingleSession(t *testing.T, dir string, checkpointID string) {
   171→	t.Helper()
   172→	meta := ReadCheckpointMetadata(t, dir, checkpointID)
   173→	assert.Len(t, meta.Sessions, 1,
   174→		"expected 1 session in checkpoint %s, got %d", checkpointID, len(meta.Sessions))
   175→}
   176→
   177→// AssertCheckpointMetadataComplete asserts essential fields in checkpoint metadata are populated.
   178→func AssertCheckpointMetadataComplete(t *testing.T, dir string, checkpointID string) {
   179→	t.Helper()
   180→	meta := ReadCheckpointMetadata(t, dir, checkpointID)
   181→	assert.NotEmpty(t, meta.CLIVersion, "checkpoint %s: cli_version should be set", checkpointID)
   182→	assert.NotEmpty(t, meta.Strategy, "checkpoint %s: strategy should be set", checkpointID)
   183→	assert.NotEmpty(t, meta.Sessions, "checkpoint %s: should have at least 1 session", checkpointID)
   184→	assert.Equal(t, checkpointID, meta.CheckpointID,
   185→		"checkpoint metadata ID should match expected")
   186→}
   187→
   188→// AssertCheckpointFilesTouched asserts the checkpoint metadata lists exactly
   189→// the expected files in files_touched (order-independent).
   190→func AssertCheckpointFilesTouched(t *testing.T, dir string, checkpointID string, expected []string) {
   191→	t.Helper()
   192→	meta := ReadCheckpointMetadata(t, dir, checkpointID)
   193→	assert.ElementsMatch(t, expected, meta.FilesTouched,
   194→		"checkpoint %s: files_touched mismatch", checkpointID)
   195→}
   196→
   197→// shadowBranches returns all shadow branches (entire/*) excluding entire/checkpoints/*.
   198→func shadowBranches(t *testing.T, dir string) []string {
   199→	t.Helper()
   200→	branches := GitOutput(t, dir, "for-each-ref", "--format=%(refname:short)", "refs/heads/entire/")
   201→	var shadow []string
   202→	for _, b := range strings.Split(branches, "\n") {
   203→		b = strings.TrimSpace(b)
   204→		if b == "" || strings.HasPrefix(b, "entire/checkpoints") {
   205→			continue
   206→		}
   207→		shadow = append(shadow, b)
   208→	}
   209→	return shadow
   210→}
   211→
   212→// AssertNoShadowBranches asserts that no shadow branches (entire/*) remain,
   213→// excluding entire/checkpoints/*.
   214→func AssertNoShadowBranches(t *testing.T, dir string) {
   215→	t.Helper()
   216→	shadow := shadowBranches(t, dir)
   217→	assert.Empty(t, shadow,
   218→		"shadow branches should be cleaned up after commit, found: %v", shadow)
   219→}
   220→
   221→// AssertHasShadowBranches asserts that at least one shadow branch exists.
   222→func AssertHasShadowBranches(t *testing.T, dir string) {
   223→	t.Helper()
   224→	shadow := shadowBranches(t, dir)
   225→	assert.NotEmpty(t, shadow,
   226→		"expected at least one shadow branch to persist, but none found")
   227→}
   228→
   229→// WaitForSessionIdle polls the session state files in .git/entire-sessions/
   230→// until no session has phase "active", or fails the test after timeout.
   231→func WaitForSessionIdle(t *testing.T, dir string, timeout time.Duration) {
   232→	t.Helper()
   233→	stateDir := filepath.Join(dir, ".git", "entire-sessions")
   234→	deadline := time.Now().Add(timeout)
   235→	for time.Now().Before(deadline) {
   236→		entries, err := os.ReadDir(stateDir)
   237→		if err != nil {
   238→			time.Sleep(200 * time.Millisecond)
   239→			continue
   240→		}
   241→		anyActive := false
   242→		for _, entry := range entries {
   243→			if entry.IsDir() || !strings.HasSuffix(entry.Name(), ".json") || strings.HasSuffix(entry.Name(), ".tmp") {
   244→				continue
   245→			}
   246→			data, err := os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(stateDir, entry.Name()))
   247→			if err != nil {
   248→				continue
   249→			}
   250→			var state struct {
   251→				Phase string `json:"phase"`
   252→			}
   253→			if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &state); err != nil {
   254→				continue
   255→			}
   256→			if state.Phase == "active" {
   257→				anyActive = true
   258→				break
   259→			}
   260→		}
   261→		if !anyActive {
   262→			return
   263→		}
   264→		time.Sleep(200 * time.Millisecond)
   265→	}
   266→	t.Fatalf("session(s) did not transition to idle within %s", timeout)
   267→}
   268→
   269→// ValidateCheckpointDeep performs comprehensive validation of checkpoint metadata
   270→// on the checkpoint branch, including transcript JSONL validity, content hash
   271→// verification, and prompt content checking.
   272→func ValidateCheckpointDeep(t *testing.T, dir string, v DeepCheckpointValidation) {
   273→	t.Helper()
   274→
   275→	AssertCheckpointExists(t, dir, v.CheckpointID)
   276→	AssertCheckpointMetadataComplete(t, dir, v.CheckpointID)
   277→
   278→	if v.Strategy != "" {
   279→		meta := ReadCheckpointMetadata(t, dir, v.CheckpointID)
   280→		assert.Equal(t, v.Strategy, meta.Strategy,
   281→			"checkpoint %s: strategy mismatch", v.CheckpointID)
   282→	}
   283→
   284→	if len(v.FilesTouched) > 0 {
   285→		AssertCheckpointFilesTouched(t, dir, v.CheckpointID, v.FilesTouched)
   286→	}
   287→
   288→	path := CheckpointPath(v.CheckpointID)
   289→
   290→	// Validate session metadata exists and has checkpoint_id
   291→	sessionBlob := fmt.Sprintf("entire/checkpoints/v1:%s/0/metadata.json", path)
   292→	sessionRaw := gitOutputSafe(dir, "show", sessionBlob)
   293→	if assert.NotEmpty(t, sessionRaw, "session metadata should exist at %s", sessionBlob) {
   294→		var sessionMeta map[string]any
   295→		if assert.NoError(t, json.Unmarshal([]byte(sessionRaw), &sessionMeta)) {
   296→			assert.Equal(t, v.CheckpointID, sessionMeta["checkpoint_id"],
   297→				"session metadata checkpoint_id should match")
   298→			assert.NotEmpty(t, sessionMeta["created_at"], "session metadata should have created_at")
   299→		}
   300→	}
   301→
   302→	// Validate transcript is valid JSONL
   303→	transcriptBlob := fmt.Sprintf("entire/checkpoints/v1:%s/0/full.jsonl", path)
   304→	transcriptRaw := gitOutputSafe(dir, "show", transcriptBlob)
   305→	if assert.NotEmpty(t, transcriptRaw, "transcript should exist at %s", transcriptBlob) {
   306→		lines := strings.Split(transcriptRaw, "\n")
   307→		nonEmpty := 0
   308→		for _, line := range lines {
   309→			if strings.TrimSpace(line) != "" {
   310→				nonEmpty++
   311→			}
   312→		}
   313→		assert.Positive(t, nonEmpty, "transcript should have at least one line")
   314→
   315→		for _, expected := range v.ExpectedTranscriptContent {
   316→			assert.Contains(t, transcriptRaw, expected,
   317→				"transcript should contain %q", expected)
   318→		}
   319→
   320→		// Validate content hash
   321→		hashBlob := fmt.Sprintf("entire/checkpoints/v1:%s/0/content_hash.txt", path)
   322→		hashRaw := gitOutputSafe(dir, "show", hashBlob)
   323→		if hashRaw != "" {
   324→			hash := sha256.Sum256([]byte(transcriptRaw))
   325→			expectedHash := "sha256:" + hex.EncodeToString(hash[:])
   326→			assert.Equal(t, expectedHash, strings.TrimSpace(hashRaw),
   327→				"content hash should match transcript SHA-256")
   328→		}
   329→	}
   330→
   331→	// Validate prompt.txt if expected prompts specified
   332→	if len(v.ExpectedPrompts) > 0 {
   333→		promptBlob := fmt.Sprintf("entire/checkpoints/v1:%s/0/prompt.txt", path)
   334→		promptRaw := gitOutputSafe(dir, "show", promptBlob)
   335→		for _, expected := range v.ExpectedPrompts {
   336→			assert.Contains(t, promptRaw, expected,
   337→				"prompt.txt should contain %q", expected)
   338→		}
   339→	}
   340→}
   341→

     5→import (

    10→	"time"

    17→
    18→// TestLifecycle_SinglePromptManualCommit verifies the basic flow:
    19→// agent creates a file → git add + commit → checkpoint exists with trailer.
    20→func TestLifecycle_SinglePromptManualCommit(t *testing.T) {
    21→	testutil.ForEachAgent(t, 2*time.Minute, func(t *testing.T, s *testutil.RepoState, ctx context.Context) {
    22→		_, err := s.RunPrompt(t, ctx, "Create a file called hello.txt with the content 'hello world'. Do not ask for confirmation.")
    23→		require.NoError(t, err, "prompt failed")
    24→
    25→		testutil.AssertFileExists(t, s.Dir, "hello.txt")
    26→
    27→		s.Git(t, "add", ".")
    28→		s.Git(t, "commit", "-m", "add hello.txt")
    29→
    30→		testutil.WaitForCheckpoint(t, s, 30*time.Second)
    31→
    32→		cpID := testutil.AssertHasCheckpointTrailer(t, s.Dir, "HEAD")
    33→		testutil.AssertCheckpointExists(t, s.Dir, cpID)
    34→	})
    35→}
    36→
    37→// TestLifecycle_MultiplePromptsManualCommit sends two prompts, commits once,
    38→// and verifies the checkpoint covers both files.
    39→func TestLifecycle_MultiplePromptsManualCommit(t *testing.T) {
    40→	testutil.ForEachAgent(t, 3*time.Minute, func(t *testing.T, s *testutil.RepoState, ctx context.Context) {
    41→		_, err := s.RunPrompt(t, ctx, "Create a file called foo.txt containing 'foo'. Do not ask for confirmation.")
    42→		require.NoError(t, err, "first prompt failed")
    43→
    44→		_, err = s.RunPrompt(t, ctx, "Create a file called bar.txt containing 'bar'. Do not ask for confirmation.")
    45→		require.NoError(t, err, "second prompt failed")
    46→
    47→		testutil.AssertFileExists(t, s.Dir, "foo.txt")
    48→		testutil.AssertFileExists(t, s.Dir, "bar.txt")
    49→
    50→		s.Git(t, "add", ".")
    51→		s.Git(t, "commit", "-m", "add foo and bar")
    52→
    53→		testutil.WaitForCheckpoint(t, s, 30*time.Second)
    54→		testutil.AssertCheckpointAdvanced(t, s)
    55→	})
    56→}
    57→
    58→// TestLifecycle_DetectAndEnable verifies that `entire enable --agent kiro`
    59→// works when .kiro/ already exists in the repo.
    60→func TestLifecycle_DetectAndEnable(t *testing.T) {
    61→	testutil.ForEachAgent(t, 1*time.Minute, func(t *testing.T, s *testutil.RepoState, _ context.Context) {
    62→		// The repo is already set up with entire enable by ForEachAgent/SetupRepo.
    63→		// Verify .entire/ directory exists.
    64→		_, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(s.Dir, ".entire"))
    65→		require.NoError(t, err, "expected .entire/ directory after enable")
    66→	})
    67→}
    68→
    69→// TestLifecycle_HooksInstalledAfterEnable verifies that hooks are installed
    70→// after running `entire enable`.
    71→func TestLifecycle_HooksInstalledAfterEnable(t *testing.T) {
    72→	testutil.ForEachAgent(t, 1*time.Minute, func(t *testing.T, s *testutil.RepoState, _ context.Context) {
    73→		agentBinName := "entire-agent-" + s.Agent.EntireAgent()
    74→		binPath, ok := AgentBinaries[agentBinName]
    75→		if !ok {
    76→			t.Skipf("%s binary not built", agentBinName)
    77→		}
    78→
    79→		runner := &AgentRunner{
    80→			BinaryPath: binPath,
    81→			Env: []string{
    82→				"ENTIRE_REPO_ROOT=" + s.Dir,
    83→				"HOME=" + os.Getenv("HOME"),
    84→				"PATH=" + os.Getenv("PATH"),
    85→				"LANG=en_US.UTF-8",
    86→			},
    87→		}
    88→
    89→		var resp struct {
    90→			Installed bool `json:"installed"`
    91→		}
    92→		runner.RunJSON(t, &resp, "", "are-hooks-installed")
    93→
    94→		assert.True(t, resp.Installed, "hooks should be installed after entire enable")
    95→	})
    96→}
    97→
    98→// TestLifecycle_RewindPreCommit verifies pre-commit shadow branch rewind:
    99→// agent creates file A → snapshot rewind points → agent creates file B → rewind → file A exists, file B gone.
   100→// No commits happen — this tests pure shadow branch rewind points.
   101→func TestLifecycle_RewindPreCommit(t *testing.T) {
   102→	testutil.ForEachAgent(t, 3*time.Minute, func(t *testing.T, s *testutil.RepoState, ctx context.Context) {
   103→		// Agent creates file A (no commit).
   104→		_, err := s.RunPrompt(t, ctx, "Create a file called alpha.txt with content 'alpha'. Do not commit the file. Do not ask for confirmation.")
   105→		require.NoError(t, err, "first prompt failed")
   106→		testutil.AssertFileExists(t, s.Dir, "alpha.txt")
   107→
   108→		// Snapshot rewind points (shadow branch, is_logs_only=false).
   109→		points := entire.RewindList(t, s.Dir)
   110→		require.NotEmpty(t, points, "expected at least one rewind point after first prompt")
   111→		rewindTarget := points[0].ID
   112→
   113→		// Agent creates file B (no commit).
   114→		_, err = s.RunPrompt(t, ctx, "Create a file called beta.txt with content 'beta'. Do not commit the file. Do not ask for confirmation.")
   115→		require.NoError(t, err, "second prompt failed")
   116→		testutil.AssertFileExists(t, s.Dir, "beta.txt")
   117→
   118→		// Rewind to point after file A was created.
   119→		err = entire.Rewind(t, s.Dir, rewindTarget)
   120→		require.NoError(t, err, "rewind to %s should succeed", rewindTarget)
   121→
   122→		// File A should still exist, file B should be gone.
   123→		_, err = os.Stat(filepath.Join(s.Dir, "alpha.txt"))
   124→		assert.NoError(t, err, "alpha.txt should exist after rewind")
   125→		_, err = os.Stat(filepath.Join(s.Dir, "beta.txt"))
   126→		assert.True(t, os.IsNotExist(err), "beta.txt should not exist after rewind")
   127→	})
   128→}
   129→
   130→// TestLifecycle_RewindAfterCommit verifies that pre-commit shadow branch
   131→// rewind points become invalid after a user commit. Rewinding to an old
   132→// shadow branch ID should fail because condensation converts them to logs-only.
   133→func TestLifecycle_RewindAfterCommit(t *testing.T) {
   134→	testutil.ForEachAgent(t, 3*time.Minute, func(t *testing.T, s *testutil.RepoState, ctx context.Context) {
   135→		// Agent creates a file (no commit).
   136→		_, err := s.RunPrompt(t, ctx, "Create a file called first.txt with content 'first'. Do not commit the file. Do not ask for confirmation.")
   137→		require.NoError(t, err, "prompt failed")
   138→		testutil.AssertFileExists(t, s.Dir, "first.txt")
   139→
   140→		// Get pre-commit rewind points and find a non-logs-only shadow point.
   141→		pointsBefore := entire.RewindList(t, s.Dir)
   142→		require.NotEmpty(t, pointsBefore, "expected rewind points before commit")
   143→
   144→		var shadowPoint *entire.RewindPoint
   145→		for i := range pointsBefore {
   146→			if !pointsBefore[i].IsLogsOnly {
   147→				shadowPoint = &pointsBefore[i]
   148→				break
   149→			}
   150→		}
   151→		require.NotNil(t, shadowPoint, "expected at least one non-logs-only shadow branch rewind point")
   152→		oldID := shadowPoint.ID
   153→
   154→		// User commits the file — triggers condensation.
   155→		s.Git(t, "add", ".")
   156→		s.Git(t, "commit", "-m", "add first.txt")
   157→		testutil.WaitForCheckpoint(t, s, 30*time.Second)
   158→
   159→		// Old shadow point should no longer be listed as non-logs-only.
   160→		pointsAfter := entire.RewindList(t, s.Dir)
   161→		found := false
   162→		for _, p := range pointsAfter {
   163→			if p.ID == oldID && !p.IsLogsOnly {
   164→				found = true
   165→				break
   166→			}
   167→		}
   168→		assert.False(t, found, "old shadow branch rewind point %s should no longer be listed as non-logs-only", oldID)
   169→
   170→		// Attempting to rewind to the old shadow branch ID should fail.
   171→		err = entire.Rewind(t, s.Dir, oldID)
   172→		assert.Error(t, err, "rewind to old shadow branch ID should fail after commit")
   173→
   174→		// Working directory should be unchanged — file still committed.
   175→		testutil.AssertFileExists(t, s.Dir, "first.txt")
   176→		testutil.AssertNoShadowBranches(t, s.Dir)
   177→	})
   178→}
   179→
   180→// TestLifecycle_SessionPersistence verifies that a session file is created in
   181→// .entire/tmp/ after running a prompt.
   182→func TestLifecycle_SessionPersistence(t *testing.T) {
   183→	testutil.ForEachAgent(t, 2*time.Minute, func(t *testing.T, s *testutil.RepoState, ctx context.Context) {
   184→		_, err := s.RunPrompt(t, ctx, "Create a file called session-test.txt with content 'test'. Do not ask for confirmation.")
   185→		require.NoError(t, err, "prompt failed")
   186→
   187→		tmpDir := filepath.Join(s.Dir, ".entire", "tmp")
   188→		entries, err := os.ReadDir(tmpDir)
   189→		require.NoError(t, err, "read .entire/tmp/")
   190→
   191→		hasSession := false
   192→		for _, entry := range entries {
   193→			if filepath.Ext(entry.Name()) == ".json" {
   194→				hasSession = true
   195→				break
   196→			}
   197→		}
   198→		assert.True(t, hasSession, "expected at least one .json session file in .entire/tmp/")
   199→	})
   200→}
   201→
   202→// TestLifecycle_InteractiveSession verifies that an interactive tmux session
   203→// can send prompts and receive responses.
   204→func TestLifecycle_InteractiveSession(t *testing.T) {
   205→	testutil.ForEachAgent(t, 3*time.Minute, func(t *testing.T, s *testutil.RepoState, ctx context.Context) {
   206→		session := s.StartSession(t, ctx)
   207→		if session == nil {
   208→			t.Skip("agent does not support interactive sessions")
   209→		}
   210→
   211→		s.Send(t, session, "Create a file called interactive.txt with 'hello'. Do not ask for confirmation.")
   212→		s.WaitFor(t, session, s.Agent.PromptPattern(), 60*time.Second)
   213→
   214→		testutil.WaitForFileExists(t, s.Dir, "interactive.txt", 10*time.Second)
   215→
   216→		s.Git(t, "add", ".")
   217→		s.Git(t, "commit", "-m", "interactive test")
   218→		testutil.WaitForCheckpoint(t, s, 30*time.Second)
   219→	})
   220→}

  "prompt": "## Context\n\nI'm fixing a bug in the kiro external agent where each checkpoint stores the FULL cumulative transcript from kiro's SQLite database, rather than just the new entries for that session.\n\n### Evidence from test repo\n- Checkpoint N-1: prompt \"rock paper scissors\", transcript 23,280 bytes\n- Checkpoint N: prompt \"yes golang\", transcript 92,300 bytes, but `full.jsonl` starts with \"rock paper scissors\"\n- Different session IDs, so it's not a session mix-up\n- Kiro stores one conversation row per CWD in its DB, with cumulative history\n\n### Root Cause\n`ensureCachedTranscript()` in `agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go:99-131` queries kiro's SQLite DB:\n```sql\nSELECT value FROM conversations_v2 WHERE key = '<cwd>' ORDER BY updated_at DESC LIMIT 1\n```\nThis returns the FULL cumulative conversation (all sessions for this CWD). The raw value is written to the cache file without trimming.\n\n### Proposed Fix\nStore a \"last captured transcript position\" in a persistent file (`.entire/tmp/kiro-transcript-offset`). In `ensureCachedTranscript`, after fetching the full transcript:\n1. Parse it to get the history entries\n2. Read the stored offset (0 if first time)\n3. Trim `history` to `history[offset:]`\n4. Re-serialize the trimmed transcript\n5. Write trimmed transcript to cache file\n6. Update the offset file with the new total position\n\n### Key Files\n- `agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go` - Main file to modify\n- `agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/types.go` - kiroTranscript struct\n- `agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/hooks.go` - Stop hook calls captureTranscriptForStop\n- `agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/agent.go` - ReadSession uses the cached file\n- `agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/paths.go` - Session dir/file resolution\n- `agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/protocol/protocol.go` - RepoRoot(), DefaultSessionDir()\n\n### Existing Functions to Reuse\n- `parseTranscript(data []byte) (*kiroTranscript, error)` - already parses transcript JSON\n- `kiroTranscript` struct has `ConversationID` and `History []kiroHistoryEntry`\n- `cacheTranscriptPath(cwd, sessionID)` - resolves cache file path\n- `protocol.RepoRoot()` and `protocol.DefaultSessionDir()` for path resolution\n- `json.Marshal` for re-serialization\n\n### Constraints\n- Each session generates a unique sessionID (in agent-spawn hook)\n- The Entire CLI calls `read-session` and `read-transcript` after the stop hook\n- `GetTranscriptPosition`, `ExtractPrompts`, `ExtractModifiedFiles` all read from the cached file\n- After trimming, all these functions should work correctly with offset=0\n- The `ensureIDETranscript` path might have the same issue and should be handled similarly\n\n### Edge Cases to Consider\n1. Now I have a thorough understanding of the codebase. Let me design the implementation plan.

## Implementation Plan

### Problem Summary

`ensureCachedTranscript()` fetches the full cumulative conversation from kiro's SQLite DB and writes it verbatim to the cache file. Each checkpoint therefore contains the entire conversation history (all sessions), not just the new entries for the current session. This causes checkpoint sizes to balloon and includes stale data from prior sessions.

### Design

**Approach: Offset file tracking with conversation_id awareness**

Store a persistent offset file at `.entire/tmp/kiro-transcript-offset.json` that records:
- The `conversation_id` last seen
- The number of history entries already captured

On each `ensureCachedTranscript` call, fetch the full transcript, parse it, compare against the stored offset, slice `history[offset:]`, re-serialize, and write the trimmed version to the cache file. Then update the offset file with the new total length.

### Step-by-step Implementation

**1. Add an offset state type and helper functions in `transcript.go`**

Add a small struct for the offset file:

```go
type transcriptOffset struct {
    ConversationID string `json:"conversation_id"`
    HistoryLength  int    `json:"history_length"`
}
```

Add two helper functions:
- `readTranscriptOffset(path string) (*transcriptOffset, error)` - reads and parses the offset file; returns zero-value offset if file does not exist.
- `writeTranscriptOffset(path string, offset *transcriptOffset) error` - writes the offset file atomically.

The offset file path should be: `filepath.Join(repoRoot, ".entire", "tmp", "kiro-transcript-offset.json")`. Add a method `(a *Agent) transcriptOffsetPath() string` that computes this, mirroring the pattern from `sessionIDCachePath()`.

**2. Modify `ensureCachedTranscript` to trim history**

Current flow (lines 99-131):
1. Query SQLite for full transcript
2. Write raw string to cache file

New flow:
1. Query SQLite for full transcript (unchanged)
2. Parse the transcript using `parseTranscript()`
3. Read the offset file via `readTranscriptOffset()`
4. If `conversation_id` matches and `offset.HistoryLength <= len(transcript.History)`, trim: `transcript.History = transcript.History[offset.HistoryLength:]`
5. If `conversation_id` differs or `offset.HistoryLength > len(transcript.History)` (conversation was cleared/reset), use the full transcript and set offset to 0
6. Re-serialize with `json.Marshal(transcript)`
7. Write trimmed JSON to cache file
8. Update offset file with new total length: `{conversation_id, original_full_length}`

Key detail: the offset file must store the total length of the **full** transcript (before trimming), not the length of the trimmed portion. This ensures the next invocation knows where the previous capture ended.

**3. Handle `ensureIDETranscript` similarly**

`ensureIDETranscript` reads from per-session JSON files in the IDE workspace directory. Each file is a single session's transcript (selected by `dateCreated` sort). This means the IDE path likely does NOT have the same cumulative problem since it reads a specific session file, not a shared conversation row. However, to be safe and consistent:

- After reading the IDE transcript, also parse it, apply the same offset logic, and trim.
- Use the same offset file (or a separate one like `kiro-ide-transcript-offset.json` if we want isolation).

**Recommendation**: After inspecting `ensureIDETranscript`, each IDE session file is already scoped to that session. The sessions index is sorted by `dateCreated` and the latest is picked. Since each session file is self-contained, trimming is likely unnecessary for the IDE path. I would add a comment documenting this reasoning and skip the trimming for `ensureIDETranscript`.

**4. Ensure downstream consumers work correctly**

After trimming, the cached file will contain only the new entries for this session. Let me verify each consumer:

- `GetTranscriptPosition(path)` at line 254: returns `len(transcript.History)` from the cached file. After trimming, this returns the count of new entries only. This is correct because the Entire CLI uses this as a position within the cached file, not within the full kiro conversation.

- `ExtractModifiedFiles(path, offset)` at line 265: reads from cache, applies `offset` to slice history. After trimming, offset should be 0 for a fresh read of the cached file. The caller passes offsets relative to the cached file, so this works.

- `ExtractPrompts(path, offset)` at line 282: same pattern, works with cached file offsets.

- `ExtractSummary(path)` at line 300: reads last assistant response from history. After trimming, this returns the last response from the current session only. This is the correct behavior.

- `ReadSession` in `agent.go` line 54: reads `sessionRef` (the cached file) as raw bytes into `NativeData`. After trimming, this contains only the current session's data. Correct.

All consumers operate on the cached file contents, so trimming at write time is transparent and correct.

**5. Handle the offset update atomicity concern**

The concern from the requirements: "Failed checkpoint creation - offset is updated but checkpoint not stored."

The offset should be updated **after** the cache file is successfully written. The sequence in `ensureCachedTranscript` should be:
1. Parse and trim
2. Write cache file
3. Update offset file

If step 2 fails, the offset is not updated, so the next attempt will re-capture the same entries (safe, just duplicated).
If step 3 fails after step 2 succeeds, the next attempt will re-capture entries that were already written to the cache (also safe, just duplicated). Duplication is much safer than data loss.

**6. Reset offset on `agent-spawn` hook**

Consider whether to reset the offset file when a new session starts (the `agent-spawn` hook). This is NOT needed because the offset file tracks `conversation_id`. If kiro starts a new conversation (different `conversation_id`), the offset logic already falls through to "use full transcript." If it reuses the same conversation, the offset correctly tracks the cumulative position.

However, there is a subtle issue: if a user starts a new session but kiro appends to the same conversation (same `conversation_id`), the offset correctly captures only the new entries. This is the desired behavior.

### Unit Test Strategy

**Test 1: `TestEnsureCachedTranscriptTrimsHistory`**
- Set up a stub SQLite runner returning a transcript with 6 history entries
- Pre-seed an offset file with `{conversation_id: "conv-1", history_length: 4}`
- Call `ensureCachedTranscript`
- Verify the cached file contains only entries 4-5 (the last 2)
- Verify the offset file is updated to `{conversation_id: "conv-1", history_length: 6}`

**Test 2: `TestEnsureCachedTranscriptFirstSession`**
- No offset file exists
- Call `ensureCachedTranscript` with a 4-entry transcript
- Verify full transcript is written to cache
- Verify offset file is created with `history_length: 4`

**Test 3: `TestEnsureCachedTranscriptConversationIDChange`**
- Offset file has `conversation_id: "old-conv"`
- SQLite returns transcript with `conversation_id: "new-conv"`
- Verify full transcript is written (no trimming)
- Verify offset is reset to new conversation's length

**Test 4: `TestEnsureCachedTranscriptOffsetExceedsLength`**
- Offset file has `history_length: 10`
- SQLite returns transcript with only 3 entries (conversation was cleared)
- Verify full transcript is written
- Verify offset is reset to 3

**Test 5: Update existing `TestEnsureCachedTranscriptWritesSQLiteTranscript`**
- This test needs updating since the cached file will now be re-serialized JSON (not the raw SQLite output). The whitespace/formatting may differ. Update assertions to compare parsed structs or normalize JSON.

**Test 6: `TestTranscriptOffsetReadWriteRoundTrip`**
- Write an offset, read it back, verify equality

### Files to Modify

1. `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go` - Add offset types, helpers, and modify `ensureCachedTranscript`
2. `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript_test.go` - Add new tests, update existing test
3. `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/types.go` - Add `transcriptOffset` struct (alternatively keep it in `transcript.go` since it is private)

### Risks and Mitigations

**Risk**: Re-serializing with `json.Marshal` may not produce byte-identical output to the original SQLite value (different whitespace, key ordering). **Mitigation**: All consumers use `parseTranscript()` which does `json.Unmarshal`, so formatting differences do not matter.

**Risk**: Concurrent kiro sessions in the same repo could race on the offset file. **Mitigation**: The Entire CLI runs hooks sequentially per repo, so this should not happen in practice. If needed, a file lock could be added later.

**Risk**: The offset file grows stale if kiro is used outside of Entire. **Mitigation**: The `conversation_id` check handles this -- if the conversation advances beyond what we expect, we just capture everything from the stored offset. If the conversation resets, we detect the ID change and capture the full new conversation.

### Critical Files for Implementation
- `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go` - Core logic: add offset tracking and trimming to `ensureCachedTranscript`
- `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript_test.go` - Add new tests and update existing test for trimming behavior
- `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/types.go` - Reference for `kiroTranscript` struct; optionally add `transcriptOffset` struct here
- `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/hooks.go` - Reference for session lifecycle flow and `sessionIDCachePath` pattern to follow
- `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/protocol/protocol.go` - Reference for `DefaultSessionDir` and `RepoRoot` used in path computation

   111→func TestQuerySessionIDParsesSQLiteJSON(t *testing.T) {
   112→	repoRoot := t.TempDir()

   118→	restore := stubSQLiteRunner(t, func(args ...string) ([]byte, error) {

   357→func stubSQLiteRunner(t *testing.T, fn func(args ...string) ([]byte, error)) func() {
   358→	t.Helper()
   359→	previous := runSQLiteCommand
   360→	runSQLiteCommand = fn
   361→	return func() {
   362→		runSQLiteCommand = previous
   363→	}
   364→}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/alisha/.claude/plans/iridescent-leaping-prism.md",
  "content": "# Fix: Kiro checkpoint stores cumulative transcript instead of session delta\n\n## Context\n\nThe last checkpoint in the test kiro repo (`/Users/alisha/Projects/test-repos/kirocli`) has a `full.jsonl` that starts with \"create a program that plays rock paper scissors\" — the prompt from the **previous** checkpoint — even though this checkpoint's prompt is \"yes golang\".\n\n**Root cause**: Kiro stores one conversation row per CWD in its SQLite database (`conversations_v2`), containing the full cumulative history across all sessions. `ensureCachedTranscript()` queries this row with `SELECT value ... LIMIT 1` and writes the entire value to the cache file. Each checkpoint therefore stores the full history, not just the new session's entries.\n\n**Evidence**: Checkpoint N-1 transcript = 23,280 bytes; Checkpoint N transcript = 92,300 bytes (cumulative). Both have different content hashes but N's `full.jsonl` starts with N-1's prompt text.\n\n## Fix\n\nTrack the last captured transcript position in a persistent offset file. On each capture, trim the history to only include new entries since the last capture.\n\n### Files to Modify\n\n1. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go`** — Core changes\n2. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript_test.go`** — Update + new tests\n\n### Implementation\n\n#### 1. Add offset tracking (transcript.go)\n\nAdd a `transcriptOffset` struct (private, keep in transcript.go):\n\n```go\ntype transcriptOffset struct {\n    ConversationID string `json:\"conversation_id\"`\n    Position       int    `json:\"position\"`\n}\n```\n\nAdd three helpers following the pattern from `sessionIDCachePath()` / `readCachedSessionID()` in hooks.go:\n\n- `(a *Agent) transcriptOffsetPath() string` — returns `filepath.Join(protocol.RepoRoot(), \".entire\", \"tmp\", \"kiro-transcript-offset.json\")`\n- `readTranscriptOffset(path string) transcriptOffset` — reads and parses; returns zero-value on any error\n- `writeTranscriptOffset(path string, offset transcriptOffset) error` — marshals and writes\n\n#### 2. Modify `ensureCachedTranscript` (transcript.go:99-131)\n\nAfter fetching the raw transcript from SQLite (line 118) and before writing to cache (line 127):\n\n1. Parse the raw transcript: `parsed, err := parseTranscript([]byte(transcript))`\n2. Read stored offset: `offset := readTranscriptOffset(a.transcriptOffsetPath())`\n3. Determine trim point:\n   - If `offset.ConversationID == parsed.ConversationID` AND `offset.Position <= len(parsed.History)` → trim from `offset.Position`\n   - Otherwise (new conversation or reset) → use full history (trim from 0)\n4. Store the full position BEFORE trimming: `newOffset := transcriptOffset{ConversationID: parsed.ConversationID, Position: len(parsed.History)}`\n5. > AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Why the transcript is cumulative**: Kiro CLI stores conversations in SQLite keyed by CWD — one row per directory. Each session appends to the same conversation rather than creating a new row. The external agent queries `ORDER BY updated_at DESC LIMIT 1`, getting the ever-growing full history.

**Why offset-based trimming works here**: The transcript analysis protocol (`GetTranscriptPosition`, `ExtractPrompts`, `ExtractModifiedFiles`) already uses offsets to extract deltas from full transcripts. By trimming at capture time, each checkpoint's `full.jsonl` becomes self-contained — downstream functions work correctly with `offset=0`.

**IDE path doesn't need this fix**: IDE transcripts come from per-session files (`<sessionId>.json`) in the workspace sessions directory, so each file is already scoped to one session. Only the CLI/SQLite path has the cumulative problem.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

> DEVELOPER

how would query db fix work?

> AGENT

The SQLite query currently returns the full `value` JSON blob which contains the cumulative `history` array. There are a few ways to scope it:

**Option A: SQL-level array slicing with `json_each`**

If we know the offset (N entries already captured), we could extract only new entries in the query itself:

```sql
SELECT json_object(
  'conversation_id', json_extract(value, '$.conversation_id'),
  'history', (
    SELECT json_group_array(je.value)
    FROM json_each(json_extract(value, '$.history')) je
    WHERE je.key >= 4
  )
) FROM conversations_v2 WHERE key = '<cwd>'
ORDER BY updated_at DESC LIMIT 1
```

This slices `history[4:]` in SQL. But it still needs a stored offset to know what `4` should be — so it has the same state-tracking problem as the offset file approach, just moved to the query.

**Option B: Parse + trim in Go after the query**

Fetch the full value (same query as now), then in Go:
1. `json.Unmarshal` into `kiroTranscript`
2. Trim `history[offset:]`
3. `json.Marshal` the trimmed result

This is essentially what my original plan proposed, just clarifying that the DB query stays the same — the trimming happens in Go.

**Option C: Query by a different key/table**

If kiro's DB had per-session rows (not per-CWD), we could query for the specific session. But kiro uses `key = cwd` with one cumulative row, so this isn't available without changes to kiro itself.

In practice, A and B both need the same offset tracking — A just does the slicing in SQL while B does it in Go. B is simpler and more testable since we already have `parseTranscript()`.

Is there a different angle you had in mind, or does one of these sound right?

> DEVELOPER

what is CWD

> AGENT

CWD = Current Working Directory — the repo path. In kiro's SQLite database (`conversations_v2` table), the `key` column is the filesystem path where kiro was running (e.g., `/Users/alisha/Projects/test-repos/kirocli`). Kiro stores one conversation row per directory, and all sessions in that directory append to the same row's `history` array.

So the query `WHERE key = '<cwd>'` means "get the conversation for this repo directory."

> DEVELOPER

> AGENT

You're right — kiro **does** create a new row per session. There are **2 separate conversations** for your test repo:

| conversation_id | history entries | size |
|---|---|---|
| `8bf84abb...` (latest) | 17 | 80KB |
| `998569ef...` (older) | 8 | 45KB |

So the problem is narrower than I thought. It's not "one long history for the whole repo" — it's that **both checkpoints happened within the same kiro CLI session** (`8bf84abb`). The first checkpoint captured the conversation at 2 entries, and the second checkpoint captured the same conversation at 13 entries (now 17).

This means the simplest fix could be: **query by `conversation_id` instead of by CWD**. The `querySessionID` function already extracts the `conversation_id` from the latest row. But `ensureCachedTranscript` queries by `key = cwd`, which returns the latest row — but doesn't filter by the specific conversation_id.

If a user closes and reopens kiro between checkpoints, the new session gets a new `conversation_id` and a fresh row. The bug only affects multiple checkpoints within a single kiro session.

Does that match what you saw — you ran both prompts ("rock paper scissors" then "go fish card game") in the same kiro CLI session without closing it in between?

> AGENT

Excellent data. Timestamps are on every user prompt entry, tool results have none. The approach is clear now:

- **User prompts** always have timestamps (RFC3339 format)
- **Tool results** have no timestamps, but they always follow the prompt they belong to
- So we can find the first entry with `timestamp >= sessionStartTime` and slice from there

The key insight: if `agent-spawn` fires per-prompt-cycle (which it appears to — checkpoint `2c357c43b5d8` has 3 sessions), then the agent-spawn timestamp naturally scopes each stop hook's capture to just the current prompt's entries. No persistent offset needed.

Let me update the plan.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Why timestamp filtering is cleaner than offset tracking**: The offset approach introduces persistent state that can get out of sync with checkpoint creation (offset updated but checkpoint fails). Timestamp filtering uses the agent-spawn time — which is naturally scoped to each prompt cycle and resets automatically with each new session. No stale state to worry about.

**Why querying by conversation_id matters**: The current query `WHERE key = '<cwd>' ORDER BY updated_at DESC LIMIT 1` could return the wrong conversation if another kiro session for the same CWD was opened more recently. Querying by `conversation_id` ensures we get the exact conversation we're tracking.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

## Approved Plan:
# Fix: Kiro checkpoint stores cumulative transcript instead of session delta

## Context

Each kiro CLI session creates one row in `conversations_v2` (keyed by CWD, unique `conversation_id`). Within a session, all prompt/response cycles append to the same row's `history` array. When multiple checkpoints occur within a single kiro session, each checkpoint's `full.jsonl` contains the entire cumulative history — including entries already captured by previous checkpoints.

**Test repo evidence** (`/Users/alisha/Projects/test-repos/kirocli`):
- Same `conversation_id` (`8bf84abb...`) across both checkpoints
- CP1: 2 history entries (rock paper scissors)
- CP2: 13 history entries (includes CP1's entries + "commit", "push", "go fish card game", "yes golang")
- User prompts always have `timestamp` field; tool results don't

## Fix: Query by conversation_id + timestamp filtering

Two changes:
1. Query `conversations_v2` by `conversation_id` instead of CWD (more precise targeting)
2. Filter history entries by timestamp to only include entries from the current prompt cycle

### Files to Modify

1. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go`** — Core transcript capture changes
2. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/hooks.go`** — Cache start time in agent-spawn, pass conversation_id through stop hook
3. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript_test.go`** — Update + new tests

### Step 1: Cache session start time in agent-spawn (hooks.go)

Add a constant for the start time cache file (next to `sessionIDFile = "kiro-active-session"`):

```go
const sessionStartTimeFile = "kiro-session-start"
```

Add helpers mirroring `readCachedSessionID` / `clearCachedSessionID`:
- `(a *Agent) cacheSessionStartTime()` — writes `time.Now().UTC().Format(time.RFC3339Nano)` to `.entire/tmp/kiro-session-start`
- `(a *Agent) readCachedSessionStartTime() time.Time` — reads and parses the file; returns zero time on error
- `(a *Agent) sessionStartTimePath() string` — returns path (mirror `sessionIDCachePath()`)

In the `HookNameAgentSpawn` case of `ParseHook`, call `a.cacheSessionStartTime()` alongside `a.generateAndCacheSessionID()`.

In `clearCachedSessionID`, also clear the start time file.

### Step 2: Query by conversation_id (transcript.go)

Add a new function `ensureCachedTranscriptByConversation(cwd, sessionID, conversationID string)`:
- If `conversationID` is not empty, query:
  ```sql
  SELECT value FROM conversations_v2
  WHERE json_extract(value, '$.conversation_id') = '<conversationID>'
  ORDER BY updated_at DESC LIMIT 1
  ```
- Otherwise, fall back to the existing CWD-based query

Modify `ensureCachedTranscript` signature to accept `conversationID string` as an additional parameter. Update callers.

### Step 3: Filter by timestamp (transcript.go)

In `ensureCachedTranscript`, after fetching the raw transcript from SQLite:

1. Parse: `parsed, err := parseTranscript([]byte(transcript))`
2. Read cached start time: `startTime := a.readCachedSessionStartTime()`
3. If `startTime` is not zero, find the first history entry with `user.Timestamp >= startTime`:
   ```go
   trimFrom := 0
   if !startTime.IsZero() {
       for i, entry := range parsed.History {
           if ts, err := time.Parse(time.RFC3339Nano, entry.User.Timestamp); err == nil && !ts.Before(startTime) {
               trimFrom = i
               break
           }
       }
   }
   parsed.History = parsed.History[trimFrom:]
   ```
4. Re-serialize: `trimmedJSON, _ := json.Marshal(parsed)`
5. Write `trimmedJSON` to cache (instead of raw `transcript`)

If parsing fails, fall through to original behavior (write raw transcript). This keeps the function resilient.

### Step 4: Pass conversation_id through stop hook (hooks.go)

In the `HookNameStop` case:
- Always call `querySessionID(cwd)` to get the conversation_id (not just as fallback for sessionID)
- Pass it to `captureTranscriptForStop(cwd, sessionID, conversationID)`
- Update `captureTranscriptForStop` signature to accept and forward `conversationID`

```go
case HookNameStop:
    cwd := raw.CWD
    if cwd == "" {
        cwd = protocol.RepoRoot()
    }
    sessionID := a.readCachedSessionID()
    conversationID, _ := a.querySessionID(cwd)
    if sessionID == "" {
        if conversationID != "" {
            sessionID = conversationID
        } else {
            sessionID = fallbackStopSessionID()
        }
    }
    sessionRef := a.captureTranscriptForStop(cwd, sessionID, conversationID)
    a.clearCachedSessionID()
    ...
```

### Step 5: Update tests (transcript_test.go)

**Update existing tests** for the new `ensureCachedTranscript` signature (add `conversationID` param):
- `TestEnsureCachedTranscriptWritesSQLiteTranscript` — pass empty conversationID, update assertion for re-serialized JSON
- `TestParseHookStopPrefersSQLiteTranscript` — update expected content for re-serialized JSON

**New tests:**

**`TestEnsureCachedTranscriptFiltersByTimestamp`**:
- Stub SQLite to return a 6-entry transcript (entries at T+0s, T+10s, T+20s, T+30s, T+40s, T+50s)
- Cache session start time as T+25s
- Assert cached file contains only entries 3-5 (timestamps >= T+25s)

**`TestEnsureCachedTranscriptNoStartTime`**:
- No start time file cached
- Assert full transcript written (no filtering)

**`TestEnsureCachedTranscriptQueriesByConversationID`**:
- Pass a specific conversationID
- Verify the SQLite query includes `json_extract(value, '$.conversation_id') = '<id>'`

**`TestEnsureCachedTranscriptToolResultsIncluded`**:
- Transcript with: prompt (T+0), tool_result (no ts), prompt (T+10), tool_result (no ts)
- Start time = T+5
- Assert entries [2,3] included (prompt at T+10 and its tool result)

## Verification

1. **Unit tests**: `cd agents/entire-agent-kiro && go test ./internal/kiro/ -v -run "TestEnsureCachedTranscript|TestParseHookStop"`
2. **All kiro tests**: `cd agents/entire-agent-kiro && go test ./...`
3. **Manual check**: Build the agent, run the stop hook against the test repo DB, and verify the cached transcript only contains entries from the current prompt cycle

     1→# Writing Golang code
     2→
     3→## Code and Project Organization
     4→
     5→- Never shadow variables with `:=` in inner scopes. Use explicit assignment or rename the variable.
     6→- Reduce nesting: flip conditions and return early (happy path stays left-aligned).
     7→- Avoid side effects in `init()`. Prefer explicit initialization functions that return errors.
     8→- Do not create getters/setters unless there is real logic behind them. Direct field access is idiomatic.
     9→- Do not create interfaces preemptively. Discover interfaces from usage. Accept interfaces, return structs.
    10→- Define interfaces on the **consumer** side, not the producer side.
    11→- Return concrete types from functions, not interfaces.
    12→- Avoid `any`/`interface{}` when a more specific type or interface conveys intent.
    13→- Use generics only for data structures or functions that genuinely operate on multiple types with shared constraints. Do not use generics just because you can.
    14→- Be cautious with type embedding — it promotes all methods, potentially exposing internal APIs. Prefer explicit delegation.
    15→- Use the functional options pattern (`WithTimeout(d)`) for extensible, backward-compatible configuration APIs.
    16→- Organize packages by domain, not by technical layer. Never create packages named `util`, `common`, `helpers`, or `models`.
    17→
    18→---
    19→
    20→## Data Types
    21→
    22→- Use pointer receivers for mutation or large structs; value receivers for small immutable types. Be consistent per type.
    23→- Use named result parameters only to document intent. Avoid bare returns to prevent confusion.
    24→- Never return a nil pointer typed as an interface — it creates a non-nil interface. Return `nil` explicitly for the interface type.
    25→- Accept `io.Reader`/`io.Writer` instead of filenames to make functions testable and composable.
    26→- Validate integer ranges before arithmetic to prevent silent overflow. Use `math.MaxInt` checks.
    27→- Never compare floats with `==`. Use epsilon comparisons or `math/big` for precision-sensitive code.
    28→- Understand slice length vs capacity. Appending beyond capacity copies to a new backing array.
    29→- Use full slice expressions `s[a:b:c]` to prevent shared-memory bugs when sub-slicing.
    30→- Pre-allocate slices with `make([]T, 0, n)` when size is known.
    31→- Check slice emptiness with `len(s) == 0`, never `s == nil`.
    32→- Use `copy()` or `append([]T{}, src...)` to avoid aliasing bugs from shared underlying arrays.
    33→- When sub-slicing a large array, copy needed elements to a new slice to allow the original to be GC'd.
    34→- Provide a size hint with `make(map[K]V, n)` when entry count is known.
    35→- Maps never shrink. Re-create the map if you add then delete many keys.
    36→- Structs with slices or maps cannot use `==`. Use `reflect.DeepEqual` or custom comparison.
    37→
    38→---
    39→
    40→## Control Structures
    41→
    42→- Always use the comma-ok idiom `v, ok := m[key]` to distinguish missing keys from zero values.
    43→- In Go < 1.22, range loop variables are reused. Copy the variable or use the index when taking addresses. In Go 1.22+, this is fixed.
    44→- The range expression is evaluated once before the loop. Modifications during iteration don't affect loop bounds for arrays/slices.
    45→- `break` inside `select`/`switch` only exits that block, not an enclosing `for` loop. Use labeled breaks.
    46→- Never use `defer` inside a loop body. Extract the loop body into a separate function or manage resources manually.
    47→
    48→---
    49→
    50→## Strings
    51→
    52→- A `rune` is a Unicode code point (`int32`). Use `for _, r := range s` to iterate runes, not byte indexing.
    53→- `strings.TrimRight` removes any trailing characters in a set; `strings.TrimSuffix` removes a specific suffix. Know the difference.
    54→- Use `strings.Builder` for concatenating strings in loops. Never use `+` in a loop.
    55→- Avoid unnecessary `[]byte` ↔ `string` conversions. The `strings` and `bytes` packages mirror each other.
    56→- Substrings share the parent string's memory. Use `strings.Clone()` (Go 1.20+) to allow the original to be GC'd.
    57→
    58→---
    59→
    60→## Functions and Methods
    61→
    62→- Choose receiver type deliberately and stay consistent per type. Document the choice if non-obvious.
    63→
    64→---
    65→
    66→## Error Management
    67→
    68→- Use `panic` only for unrecoverable programmer errors, never for expected runtime errors.
    69→- Wrap errors with `fmt.Errorf("context: %w", err)` when callers need `errors.Is`/`errors.As`. Use `%v` to hide implementation details.
    70→- Use `errors.As` for type checks and `errors.Is` for value checks on wrapped errors. Never use `==` or type assertions directly.
    71→- Log **or** return an error, never both. Doing both creates duplicate noise.
    72→- Always handle errors. If intentionally ignoring, assign to `_` with a comment explaining why.
    73→- Handle errors from deferred `Close()` calls — assign to a named return parameter or log explicitly.
    74→
    75→---
    76→
    77→## Concurrency: Foundations
    78→
    79→- Concurrency ≠ parallelism. Concurrency structures code; parallelism executes it simultaneously.
    80→- Don't assume concurrency is faster. Goroutine overhead and contention can make it slower. Benchmark first.
```

}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go",
  "old_string": "func (a *Agent) ensureIDETranscript(cwd string, sessionID string) (string, error) {",
  "new_string": "// filterTranscriptByStartTime parses the raw transcript and trims history\n// entries that predate the cached session start time. This prevents cumulative\n// kiro conversations from including turns that were already checkpointed.\n// Returns the re-serialized JSON and true if filtering was applied, or\n// (nil, false) if no filtering was needed or parsing failed.\nfunc (a *Agent) filterTranscriptByStartTime(raw []byte) ([]byte, bool) {\n\tstartTime := a.readCachedSessionStartTime()\n\tif startTime.IsZero() {\n\t\treturn nil, false\n\t}\n\n\tparsed, err := parseTranscript(raw)\n\tif err != nil || len(parsed.History) == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil, false\n\t}\n\n\ttrimFrom := 0\n\tfor i, entry := range parsed.History {\n\t\tif entry.User.Timestamp == \"\" {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tts, err := time.Parse(time.RFC3339Nano, entry.User.Timestamp)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !ts.Before(startTime) {\n\t\t\ttrimFrom = i\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif trimFrom == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil, false\n\t}\n\n\tparsed.History = parsed.History[trimFrom:]\n\tfiltered, err := json.Marshal(parsed)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, false\n\t}\n\treturn filtered, true\n}\n\nfunc (a *Agent) ensureIDETranscript(cwd string, sessionID string) (string, error) {"
}

```
All tests pass. Now I have a thorough understanding of the implementation. Here is my review.

---

## Code Review: Cumulative Transcript Bug Fix

### What Was Done Well

- The implementation follows the plan closely across all three files with no unjustified deviations.
- SQL injection is properly handled via `escapeSQLString` for the `conversationID` parameter, consistent with the existing `cwd` query.
- The `filterTranscriptByStartTime` function is cleanly separated from `ensureCachedTranscript`, making it independently testable and keeping the cache function simple.
- Graceful degradation: when parsing fails or no start time is cached, the function falls through to the original unfiltered behavior.
- Tests cover the four planned scenarios: filtering by timestamp, no start time, conversation_id query, and tool results inclusion.
- `clearCachedSessionID` correctly clears both the session ID and start time files, which prevents stale state from leaking across sessions.

### Critical Issues (must fix)

**1. `filterTranscriptByStartTime` silently returns the full transcript when the first timestamped entry is already >= startTime**

In `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go`, lines 164-181:

```go
trimFrom := 0
for i, entry := range parsed.History {
    if entry.User.Timestamp == "" {
        continue
    }
    ts, err := time.Parse(time.RFC3339Nano, entry.User.Timestamp)
    if err != nil {
        continue
    }
    if !ts.Before(startTime) {
        trimFrom = i
        break
    }
}

if trimFrom == 0 {
    return nil, false
}
```

When all entries are >= startTime (i.e., this is the first checkpoint in the session), `trimFrom` remains 0 and the function returns `(nil, false)`. This is actually *correct behavior* -- no filtering is needed because there is nothing to trim. However, the logic conflates two cases in the `trimFrom == 0` check: (a) every entry has timestamps >= startTime (no trimming needed -- correct), and (b) no entry has a parseable timestamp at all (cannot filter -- correct to bail). So while the end result is correct in both cases, the intent is ambiguous.

This is not actually a bug since returning the full transcript when nothing needs trimming is the right behavior. Downgrading to a suggestion below.

### Important Issues (should fix)

**2. The `filterTranscriptByStartTime` loop never reaches the case where ALL timestamped entries are BEFORE startTime**

If every timestamped entry in the transcript has a timestamp strictly before `startTime`, the loop completes without ever setting `trimFrom` (it stays 0), and the function returns `(nil, false)` -- writing the full unfiltered transcript. This means: if a new session starts and somehow the DB still has stale data from a prior session where every entry predates the current session start, the agent would include all of those stale entries.

In practice this is likely rare (a new kiro session would add new entries), but it violates the intent of the feature. Consider handling this by detecting that the loop finished without a match and returning an empty history instead.

**3. Race condition between `cacheSessionStartTime` and `readCachedSessionStartTime`**

In `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/hooks.go`, the start time is written using `time.Now().UTC()` at agent-spawn (line 463), but the kiro CLI session might not record its first history entry with a matching timestamp. If there is clock skew or if the spawn hook runs slightly before kiro records the first entry, the cached start time could be slightly before or after the first entry's timestamp, potentially including or excluding one extra entry.

This is inherent to the design (the plan acknowledges that timestamps come from kiro, not from the agent), but worth noting. A small buffer (subtracting a few hundred milliseconds from the start time) could make this more resilient.

### Suggestions (nice to have)

**4. Clarify the `trimFrom == 0` ambiguity with a comment or boolean flag**

In `filterTranscriptByStartTime`, consider using a `found` boolean alongside `trimFrom` to make the intent explicit:

```go
trimFrom := 0
found := false
for i, entry := range parsed.History {
    // ...
    if !ts.Before(startTime) {
        trimFrom = i
        found = true
        break
    }
}
if !found || trimFrom == 0 {
    return nil, false
}
```

This makes it clear that "trimFrom == 0 because the first entry matched" and "trimFrom == 0 because nothing matched" are both handled as "no filtering needed."

**5. The `TestParseHookStopPrefersSQLiteTranscript` stub dispatches on arg count, which is fragile**

In `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript_test.go` lines 262-279, the stub uses `switch len(args)` to distinguish between `querySessionID` (3 args) and `ensureCachedTranscript` (2 args). If either function's sqlite invocation changes (e.g., adding `-json` to the transcript query), this test breaks without a clear error message. Consider checking the query string content instead of relying on argument count.

**6. The `TestEnsureCachedTranscriptNoStartTimeWritesFull` test verifies count but not content identity**

This test confirms 4 entries are present but does not assert that the written data matches the original transcript data byte-for-byte. Since no filtering should occur, asserting `bytes.Equal(data, transcriptData)` would be a stronger check. Currently, a bug in re-serialization could silently change the transcript content while preserving the entry count.

**7. `buildTimestampedTranscript` uses `time.RFC3339` in the prompt text but `time.RFC3339Nano` for the timestamp field**

This is not a bug, but the test assertions on lines 672 and 791 rely on the `time.RFC3339` formatted string in the prompt content (e.g., checking for "22:00:20"). If the time format in `buildTimestampedTranscript` changes, these assertions break. Consider asserting on the `Timestamp` field directly instead of parsing the prompt text.

### Plan Alignment

The implementation matches the plan in all material aspects:

- Step 1 (cache session start time): Implemented exactly as planned with `cacheSessionStartTime`, `readCachedSessionStartTime`, and `sessionStartTimePath`.
- Step 2 (query by conversation_id): Implemented as planned with the conditional query in `ensureCachedTranscript`.
- Step 3 (filter by timestamp): Implemented as planned, with the addition of `filterTranscriptByStartTime` as a separate method (plan suggested inline code, but separation is a good improvement).
- Step 4 (pass conversation_id through stop hook): Implemented exactly as planned.
- Step 5 (tests): All four planned test cases are present plus the existing tests are updated.

One minor deviation: the plan suggested `ensureCachedTranscriptByConversation` as a new function name, but the implementation instead added `conversationID` as a parameter to the existing `ensureCachedTranscript`. This is a reasonable simplification that avoids code duplication.

### Summary

The implementation is solid and closely follows the plan. The most important issue to consider is item #2 (the case where all entries predate startTime). The other items are suggestions for robustness and test clarity. All tests pass.

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**The three-way branch in `filterTranscriptByStartTime`**: The function has three distinct outcomes: (1) `trimFrom < 0` — all entries predate start time, return empty history; (2) `trimFrom == 0` — first entry already matches, no trimming needed; (3) `trimFrom > 0` — slice from the match point. Using `-1` as sentinel instead of `0` avoids conflating "nothing to trim" with "everything to trim."

**Why `clearCachedSessionID` clears the start time too**: Both files form a session's ephemeral state. If we kept the start time after clearing the session ID, a later stop hook (without a preceding agent-spawn) would filter against a stale time from a previous session — potentially trimming entries that should be included.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Here's a summary of the changes:

**hooks.go** (+36 lines):
- Added `sessionStartTimeFile` constant and three helpers (`cacheSessionStartTime`, `readCachedSessionStartTime`, `sessionStartTimePath`)
- Agent-spawn hook now caches the start time alongside the session ID
- Stop hook always queries `conversation_id` via `querySessionID` and passes it through to `captureTranscriptForStop`
- `clearCachedSessionID` also clears the start time file

**transcript.go** (+83 lines):
- `ensureCachedTranscript` now accepts a `conversationID` parameter and queries by it when available (falling back to CWD-based query)
- New `filterTranscriptByStartTime` method: parses the transcript, finds the first entry with `timestamp >= startTime`, and slices from there
- Three cases: all entries before start time → empty history; first entry matches → no filtering; otherwise → trim
- Graceful fallback: if parsing fails or no start time cached, returns the full unfiltered transcript

**transcript_test.go** (+280 lines):
- Updated existing test stubs to handle the new `querySessionID` call in the stop hook
- 5 new tests: timestamp filtering, all-entries-before-start-time, no-start-time, conversation_id query, and tool-results-included

> AGENT

Found the root cause. The blackjack prompt timestamp is `23:49:21.943` but agent-spawn fires at `23:49:22.009` — **66ms later**. Kiro records the prompt timestamp BEFORE firing the agent-spawn hook. So the agent-spawn cached start time is always slightly AFTER the current prompt's timestamp, causing the timestamp filter to exclude the current prompt's entry entirely.

This is a fundamental flaw in the timestamp approach — agent-spawn fires after kiro records the prompt, so we can never get a start time that's before the current prompt.

Now let me also check why e2e tests don't catch this:
