Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me create a comprehensive summary of my findings.

## Summary of E2E Test Transcript Validation

Based on my thorough review of the e2e test code, here's what I found:

### 1. **e2e/lifecycle_test.go - Does NOT validate transcript content**

The lifecycle tests only use three checkpoint assertions:
- **`WaitForCheckpoint()`** - polls until the checkpoint branch advances (line 30, 53, 157, 218)
- **`AssertHasCheckpointTrailer()`** - verifies the commit has an Entire-Checkpoint trailer (line 32)
- **`AssertCheckpointExists()`** - validates only metadata.json exists (line 33)

None of the 7 tests in lifecycle_test.go check for transcript content or the existence of `full.jsonl`.

### 2. **e2e/testutil/assertions.go - Has transcript validation BUT it's never used**

The file defines a comprehensive function `ValidateCheckpointDeep()` (lines 269-340) that:
- Checks that `full.jsonl` exists at `entire/checkpoints/v1:<checkpointID>/0/full.jsonl`
- Validates transcript is not empty (at least one non-empty line)
- Checks for expected transcript content
- Validates content hash matches SHA-256
- Verifies prompt.txt content

**However**, `ValidateCheckpointDeep()` is **defined but never called** by any test. It's dead code.

### 3. **`AssertCheckpointExists()` validation (what's actually used)**

Lines 149-159 show it only checks:
1. Checkpoint ID is mentioned in the git log
2. `metadata.json` file exists on the checkpoint branch

**It does NOT check:**
- Whether `full.jsonl` exists
- Whether transcript has any content
- Anything about transcript structure

### 4. **E2E tests are NOT run in CI**

There is **no GitHub Actions workflow** (no `.github/workflows/` directory) and no other CI configuration files (.gitlab-ci.yml, .circleci/config.yml, etc.). The Makefile shows `test-e2e` and `test-e2e-lifecycle` targets exist, but there's no evidence they're run automatically in CI.

### Root Cause

The e2e tests don't fail when checkpoints have no transcripts because:
1. **No test calls `ValidateCheckpointDeep()`** - the function that would catch empty transcripts exists but is unused
2. **`AssertCheckpointExists()` only checks metadata.json** - it never touches the transcript file
3. **No CI runs these tests** - so even if the tests existed, failures wouldn't block PRs

The problem is **incomplete test coverage** - the function to validate transcripts exists but tests rely on the weaker `AssertCheckpointExists()` instead.

**Absolute file paths:**
- /Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/e2e/lifecycle_test.go
- /Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/e2e/testutil/assertions.go

Now I have the full picture. Let me update the plan to fix both issues:

1. **Timestamp filtering is fundamentally broken** — agent-spawn fires AFTER kiro records the prompt timestamp, so the filter always excludes the current prompt
2. **E2e tests don't validate transcripts** — `ValidateCheckpointDeep()` exists but is never called

The fix should switch to **offset-based trimming** (conversation_id + history position) instead of timestamps.

  "content": "# Fix: Kiro checkpoint stores cumulative transcript instead of session delta\n\n## Context\n\nEach kiro CLI session creates one row in `conversations_v2` (keyed by CWD, unique `conversation_id`). Within a session, all prompt/response cycles append to the same row's `history` array. When multiple checkpoints occur within a single kiro session, each checkpoint's `full.jsonl` contains the entire cumulative history.\n\n**Previous attempt (timestamp filtering) failed**: Kiro records the prompt timestamp BEFORE firing the agent-spawn hook (~66ms gap). So the cached start time is always slightly after the current prompt's timestamp, causing the filter to exclude the current prompt's entries entirely — resulting in empty transcripts and no checkpoints created.\n\n**E2e tests don't catch this**: `ValidateCheckpointDeep()` exists in `e2e/testutil/assertions.go` but no test calls it. Tests only use `AssertCheckpointExists()` which checks metadata.json, not `full.jsonl`.\n\n## Fix: Conversation-aware offset tracking + e2e test coverage\n\n### Part 1: Replace timestamp filtering with offset tracking\n\nStore `{conversation_id, position}` after each transcript capture. On next capture within the same conversation, trim `history[position:]`. Resets automatically when conversation_id changes (new kiro session).\n\n**Why offset > timestamp**: Offsets are exact array indices — no clock skew, no dependency on hook ordering, no approximation.\n\n### Files to Modify\n\n1. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go`** — Replace `filterTranscriptByStartTime` with offset-based trimming\n2. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/hooks.go`** — Remove session start time caching, keep conversation_id pass-through\n3. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript_test.go`** — Replace timestamp tests with offset tests\n4. **`e2e/lifecycle_test.go`** — Add `ValidateCheckpointDeep` calls to existing tests\n\n### Step 1: Add offset tracking type and helpers (transcript.go)\n\nAdd private struct and helpers:\n\n```go\ntype transcriptOffset struct {\n    ConversationID string `json:\"conversation_id\"`\n    Position       int    `json:\"position\"`\n}\n```\n\n- `(a *Agent) transcriptOffsetPath() string` — returns `.entire/tmp/kiro-transcript-offset.json` (uses `protocol.RepoRoot()`)\n- `readTranscriptOffset(path string) transcriptOffset` — reads/parses; returns zero-value on any error\n- `writeTranscriptOffset(path string, offset transcriptOffset) error` — marshal + write\n\n### Step 2: Replace `filterTranscriptByStartTime` with `trimTranscriptHistory` (transcript.go)\n\nRemove `filterTranscriptByStartTime`. Add:\n\n```go\nfunc (a *Agent) trimTranscriptHistory(raw []byte) ([]byte, bool) {\n    parsed, err := parseTranscript(raw)\n    if err != nil || len(parsed.History) == 0 {\n        return nil, false\n    }\n\n    offsetPath := a.transcriptOffsetPath()\n    prev := readTranscriptOffset(offsetPath)\n    totalLen := len(parsed.History)\n\n    // Determine trim point\n    trimFrom := 0\n    if prev.ConversationID == parsed.ConversationID && prev.Position > 0 && prev.Position <= totalLen {\n        trimFrom = prev.Position\n    }\n    // If conversation changed or offset exceeds length, capture everything (trimFrom stays 0)\n\n    // Always update the offset to the full (pre-trim) length\n    _ = writeTranscriptOffset(offsetPath, transcriptOffset{\n        ConversationID: parsed.ConversationID,\n        Position:       totalLen,\n    })\n\n    if trimFrom == 0 {\n        return nil, false  // No trimming needed — write original data\n    }\n\n    parsed.History = parsed.History[trimFrom:]\n    filtered, err := json.Marshal(parsed)\n    if err != nil {\n        return nil, false\n    }\n    return filtered, true\n}\n```\n\n### Step 3: Clean up hooks.go\n\n- **Remove**: `sessionStartTimeFile` constant, `cacheSessionStartTime()`, `readCachedSessionStartTime()`, `sessionStartTimePath()`\n- **Remove**: `a.cacheSessionStartTime()` call from agent-spawn full transcript written\n- Assert offset reset to `{conversation_id: \"conv-1\", position: 3}`\n\n**Keep**: `TestEnsureCachedTranscriptQueriesByConversationID` (still valid)\n\n### Part 2: Add > AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Why timestamp filtering failed**: Kiro's hook ordering is: (1) record prompt timestamp in conversation, (2) fire agent-spawn hook. Our code runs in step 2, so `time.Now()` is always ~50-100ms after the prompt timestamp. The filter then excludes the current prompt, producing an empty transcript.

**Why offsets are fundamentally better here**: Array indices are deterministic — entry N is always entry N regardless of clocks, timezones, or hook ordering. The conversation_id check handles session boundaries automatically (new session = new conversation_id = reset offset).

**Why e2e tests didn't catch it**: `ValidateCheckpointDeep()` validates transcripts but is dead code — no test calls it. Tests only use `AssertCheckpointExists()` which checks metadata, not transcripts.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

## Approved Plan:
# Fix: Kiro checkpoint stores cumulative transcript instead of session delta

## Context

Each kiro CLI session creates one row in `conversations_v2` (keyed by CWD, unique `conversation_id`). Within a session, all prompt/response cycles append to the same row's `history` array. When multiple checkpoints occur within a single kiro session, each checkpoint's `full.jsonl` contains the entire cumulative history.

**Previous attempt (timestamp filtering) failed**: Kiro records the prompt timestamp BEFORE firing the agent-spawn hook (~66ms gap). So the cached start time is always slightly after the current prompt's timestamp, causing the filter to exclude the current prompt's entries entirely — resulting in empty transcripts and no checkpoints created.

**E2e tests don't catch this**: `ValidateCheckpointDeep()` exists in `e2e/testutil/assertions.go` but no test calls it. Tests only use `AssertCheckpointExists()` which checks metadata.json, not `full.jsonl`.

## Fix: Conversation-aware offset tracking + e2e test coverage

### Part 1: Replace timestamp filtering with offset tracking

Store `{conversation_id, position}` after each transcript capture. On next capture within the same conversation, trim `history[position:]`. Resets automatically when conversation_id changes (new kiro session).

**Why offset > timestamp**: Offsets are exact array indices — no clock skew, no dependency on hook ordering, no approximation.

### Files to Modify

1. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go`** — Replace `filterTranscriptByStartTime` with offset-based trimming
2. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/hooks.go`** — Remove session start time caching, keep conversation_id pass-through
3. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript_test.go`** — Replace timestamp tests with offset tests
4. **`e2e/lifecycle_test.go`** — Add `ValidateCheckpointDeep` calls to existing tests

### Step 1: Add offset tracking type and helpers (transcript.go)

Add private struct and helpers:

```go
type transcriptOffset struct {
    ConversationID string `json:"conversation_id"`
    Position       int    `json:"position"`
}
```

- `(a *Agent) transcriptOffsetPath() string` — returns `.entire/tmp/kiro-transcript-offset.json` (uses `protocol.RepoRoot()`)
- `readTranscriptOffset(path string) transcriptOffset` — reads/parses; returns zero-value on any error
- `writeTranscriptOffset(path string, offset transcriptOffset) error` — marshal + write

### Step 2: Replace `filterTranscriptByStartTime` with `trimTranscriptHistory` (transcript.go)

Remove `filterTranscriptByStartTime`. Add:

```go
func (a *Agent) trimTranscriptHistory(raw []byte) ([]byte, bool) {
    parsed, err := parseTranscript(raw)
    if err != nil || len(parsed.History) == 0 {
        return nil, false
    }

    offsetPath := a.transcriptOffsetPath()
    prev := readTranscriptOffset(offsetPath)
    totalLen := len(parsed.History)

    // Determine trim point
    trimFrom := 0
    if prev.ConversationID == parsed.ConversationID && prev.Position > 0 && prev.Position <= totalLen {
        trimFrom = prev.Position
    }
    // If conversation changed or offset exceeds length, capture everything (trimFrom stays 0)

    // Always update the offset to the full (pre-trim) length
    _ = writeTranscriptOffset(offsetPath, transcriptOffset{
        ConversationID: parsed.ConversationID,
        Position:       totalLen,
    })

    if trimFrom == 0 {
        return nil, false  // No trimming needed — write original data
    }

    parsed.History = parsed.History[trimFrom:]
    filtered, err := json.Marshal(parsed)
    if err != nil {
        return nil, false
    }
    return filtered, true
}
```

### Step 3: Clean up hooks.go

- **Remove**: `sessionStartTimeFile` constant, `cacheSessionStartTime()`, `readCachedSessionStartTime()`, `sessionStartTimePath()`
- **Remove**: `a.cacheSessionStartTime()` call from agent-spawn hook
- **Remove**: `os.Remove(a.sessionStartTimePath())` from `clearCachedSessionID()`
- **Keep**: conversation_id query + pass-through in stop hook (this is still valuable)
- **Keep**: `ensureCachedTranscript` accepting `conversationID` parameter

### Step 4: Update `ensureCachedTranscript` (transcript.go)

Replace the call to `filterTranscriptByStartTime` with `trimTranscriptHistory`:

```go
data := []byte(raw)
if filtered, ok := a.trimTranscriptHistory([]byte(raw)); ok {
    data = filtered
}
```

### Step 5: Update tests (transcript_test.go)

**Remove**: `seedSessionStartTime` helper, all timestamp-related tests (`TestEnsureCachedTranscriptFiltersByTimestamp`, `TestEnsureCachedTranscriptAllEntriesBeforeStartTime`, `TestEnsureCachedTranscriptNoStartTimeWritesFull`, `TestEnsureCachedTranscriptToolResultsIncluded`)

**Add new offset-based tests:**

**`TestEnsureCachedTranscriptTrimsWithOffset`**:
- Pre-seed offset file: `{conversation_id: "conv-1", position: 4}`
- Stub returns 8-entry transcript with `conversation_id: "conv-1"`
- Assert cached file has entries 4-7 (4 entries)
- Assert offset file updated to `{conversation_id: "conv-1", position: 8}`

**`TestEnsureCachedTranscriptFirstCapture`**:
- No offset file
- Stub returns 4-entry transcript
- Assert full transcript written
- Assert offset file created with `position: 4`

**`TestEnsureCachedTranscriptConversationIDChange`**:
- Offset: `{conversation_id: "old-conv", position: 5}`
- Stub returns transcript with `conversation_id: "new-conv"`, 3 entries
- Assert full transcript written (no trimming)
- Assert offset updated to `{conversation_id: "new-conv", position: 3}`

**`TestEnsureCachedTranscriptOffsetExceedsLength`**:
- Offset: `{conversation_id: "conv-1", position: 100}`
- Stub returns 3-entry transcript with `conversation_id: "conv-1"`
- Assert full transcript written
- Assert offset reset to `{conversation_id: "conv-1", position: 3}`

**Keep**: `TestEnsureCachedTranscriptQueriesByConversationID` (still valid)

### Part 2: Add transcript validation to e2e tests

### Step 6: Use `ValidateCheckpointDeep` in lifecycle tests (e2e/lifecycle_test.go)

In `TestLifecycle_SinglePromptManualCommit`, after the existing assertions, add:

```go
testutil.ValidateCheckpointDeep(t, s.Dir, testutil.DeepCheckpointValidation{
    CheckpointID: cpID,
    FilesTouched: []string{"hello.txt"},
})
```

This uses the already-existing `ValidateCheckpointDeep` function which validates `full.jsonl` exists, has content, and the content hash matches.

## Execution Order (TDD)

### Phase A: E2e tests first (verify they fail)
1. Step 6: Add `ValidateCheckpointDeep` to lifecycle test
2. Run e2e tests — expect transcript validation failures (proves the bug is real)

### Phase B: Agent fix
3. Steps 1-5: Replace timestamp filtering with offset tracking, update unit tests
4. Run unit tests — verify they pass
5. Run e2e tests again — verify they now pass

```

The e2e test fails as expected. The transcript is `{"conversation_id":"...","history":null}` — empty history because the timestamp filtering stripped everything. This confirms the bug.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Test failure confirms the bug**: The checkpoint's transcript is `{"history":null}` — the timestamp filter removed all entries because `agent-spawn` fires after kiro records the prompt timestamp. The `ValidateCheckpointDeep` assertion caught it: transcript doesn't contain "hello".
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

  "old_string": "// filterTranscriptByStartTime parses the raw transcript and trims history\n// entries that predate the cached session start time. This prevents cumulative\n// kiro conversations from including turns that were already checkpointed.\n// Returns the re-serialized JSON and true if filtering was applied, or\n// (nil, false) if no filtering was needed or parsing failed.\nfunc (a *Agent) filterTranscriptByStartTime(raw []byte) ([]byte, bool) {\n\tstartTime := a.readCachedSessionStartTime()\n\tif startTime.IsZero() {\n\t\treturn nil, false\n\t}\n\n\tparsed, err := parseTranscript(raw)\n\tif err != nil || len(parsed.History) == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil, false\n\t}\n\n\ttrimFrom := -1\n\tfor i, entry := range parsed.History {\n\t\tif entry.User.Timestamp == \"\" {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tts, err := time.Parse(time.RFC3339Nano, entry.User.Timestamp)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !ts.Before(startTime) {\n\t\t\ttrimFrom = i\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif trimFrom < 0 {\n\t\t// All timestamped entries are before startTime — return empty history.\n\t\tparsed.History = nil\n\t} else if trimFrom == 0 {\n\t\t// First entry already matches — no trimming needed.\n\t\treturn nil, false\n\t} else {\n\t\tparsed.History = parsed.History[trimFrom:]\n\t}\n\n\tfiltered, err := json.Marshal(parsed)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, false\n\t}\n\treturn filtered, true\n}",
  "new_string": "type transcriptOffset struct {\n\tConversationID string `json:\"conversation_id\"`\n\tPosition       int    `json:\"position\"`\n}\n\nfunc (a *Agent) transcriptOffsetPath() string {\n\treturn filepath.Join(protocol.RepoRoot(), \".entire\", \"tmp\", \"kiro-transcript-offset.json\")\n}\n\nfunc readTranscriptOffset(path string) transcriptOffset {\n\tdata, err := os.ReadFile(path)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn transcriptOffset{}\n\t}\n\tvar offset transcriptOffset\n\tif err := json.Unmarshal(data, &offset); err != nil {\n\t\treturn transcriptOffset{}\n\t}\n\treturn offset\n}\n\nfunc writeTranscriptOffset(path string, offset transcriptOffset) error {\n\tif err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(path), 0o700); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tdata, err := json.Marshal(offset)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\treturn os.WriteFile(path, data, 0o600)\n}\n\n// trimTranscriptHistory trims already-checkpointed entries from a cumulative\n// kiro transcript using a stored offset. Returns the re-serialized trimmed\n// JSON and true if trimming was applied, or (nil, false) if the full\n// transcript should be used as-is.\nfunc (a *Agent) trimTranscriptHistory(raw []byte) ([]byte, bool) {\n\tparsed, err := parseTranscript(raw)\n\tif err != nil || len(parsed.History) == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil, false\n\t}\n\n\toffsetPath := a.transcriptOffsetPath()\n\tprev := readTranscriptOffset(offsetPath)\n\ttotalLen := len(parsed.History)\n\n\ttrimFrom := 0\n\tif prev.ConversationID == parsed.ConversationID && prev.Position > 0 && prev.Position <= totalLen {\n\t\ttrimFrom = prev.Position\n\t}\n\n\t// Always update the offset to the full (pre-trim) history length.\n\t_ = writeTranscriptOffset(offsetPath, transcriptOffset{\n\t\tConversationID: parsed.ConversationID,\n\t\tPosition:       totalLen,\n\t})\n\n\tif trimFrom == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil, false\n\t}\n\n\tparsed.History = parsed.History[trimFrom:]\n\tfiltered, err := json.Marshal(parsed)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, false\n\t}\n\treturn filtered, true\n}"
}

   588→// buildTimestampedTranscript creates a kiro CLI transcript with entries at known timestamps.
   589→func buildTimestampedTranscript(convID string, timestamps []time.Time) []byte {
   590→	var entries []kiroHistoryEntry
   591→	for _, ts := range timestamps {
   592→		promptJSON, _ := json.Marshal(kiroPromptContent{
   593→			Prompt: struct {
   594→				Prompt string `json:"prompt"`
   595→			}{Prompt: fmt.Sprintf("prompt at %s", ts.Format(time.RFC3339))},
   596→		})
   597→		entries = append(entries, kiroHistoryEntry{
   598→			User: kiroUserMessage{
   599→				Content:   promptJSON,
   600→				Timestamp: ts.Format(time.RFC3339Nano),
   601→			},
   602→		})
   603→		// Add a tool result entry (no timestamp) after each prompt.
   604→		entries = append(entries, kiroHistoryEntry{
   605→			User: kiroUserMessage{Content: json.RawMessage(`{"ToolUseResults":{}}`)},
   606→		})
   607→	}
   608→	transcript := kiroTranscript{ConversationID: convID, History: entries}
   609→	data, _ := json.Marshal(transcript)
   610→	return data
   611→}
   612→
   613→func seedSessionStartTime(t *testing.T, repoRoot string, ts time.Time) {

   620→		t.Fatalf("write session start time: %v", err)
   621→	}
   622→}

   624→func TestEnsureCachedTranscriptFiltersByTimestamp(t *testing.T) {

   626→	home := t.TempDir()

   633→		base.Add(10 * time.Second),    // entry 2: prompt
   634→		base.Add(20 * time.Second),    // entry 4: prompt
   635→		base.Add(30 * time.Second),    // entry 6: prompt
   636→	}
   637→	transcriptData := buildTimestampedTranscript("conv-1", timestamps)

   640→	restore := stubSQLiteRunner(t, func(args ...string) ([]byte, error) {

   643→		}
   644→		return transcriptData, nil
   645→	})
   646→	defer restore()
   647→
   648→	// Set start time between entry 2 (T+10s) and entry 4 (T+20s).
   649→	seedSessionStartTime(t, repoRoot, base.Add(15*time.Second))
   650→
   651→	cachePath, err := New().ensureCachedTranscript(repoRoot, "test-session", "")
   652→	if err != nil {
   653→		t.Fatalf("ensureCachedTranscript() error = %v", err)
   654→	}

   672→	if !strings.Contains(firstPrompt, "prompt at") || !strings.Contains(firstPrompt, "22:00:20") {
   673→		t.Fatalf("first prompt = %q, expected T+20s prompt", firstPrompt)
   674→	}
   675→}

  "new_string": "// buildTranscript creates a kiro CLI transcript with the given number of prompt entries.\nfunc buildTranscript(convID string, numPrompts int) []byte {\n\tvar entries []kiroHistoryEntry\n\tfor i := range numPrompts {\n\t\tpromptJSON, _ := json.Marshal(kiroPromptContent{\n\t\t\tPrompt: struct {\n\t\t\t\tPrompt string `json:\"prompt\"`\n\t\t\t}{Prompt: fmt.Sprintf(\"prompt %d\", i)},\n\t\t})\n\t\tentries = append(entries, kiroHistoryEntry{\n\t\t\tUser: kiroUserMessage{Content: promptJSON},\n\t\t})\n\t}\n\ttranscript := kiroTranscript{ConversationID: convID, History: entries}\n\tdata, _ := json.Marshal(transcript)\n\treturn data\n}\n\nfunc seedTranscriptOffset(t *testing.T, repoRoot string, convID string, position int) {\n\tt.Helper()\n\toffsetPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, \".entire\", \"tmp\", \"kiro-transcript-offset.json\")\n\tif err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(offsetPath), 0o750); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"mkdir: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tdata, _ := json.Marshal(transcriptOffset{ConversationID: convID, Position: position})\n\tif err := os.WriteFile(offsetPath, data, 0o600); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"write offset: %v\", err)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc readTestTranscriptOffset(t *testing.T, repoRoot string) transcriptOffset {\n\tt.Helper()\n\toffsetPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, \".entire\", \"tmp\", \"kiro-transcript-offset.json\")\n\treturn readTranscriptOffset(offsetPath)\n}\n\nfunc TestEnsureCachedTranscriptTrimsWithOffset(t *testing.T) {\n\trepoRoot := t.TempDir()\n\thome := t.TempDir()\n\tt.Setenv(\"ENTIRE_REPO_ROOT\", repoRoot)\n\tt.Setenv(\"HOME\", home)\n\n\ttranscriptData := buildTranscript(\"conv-1\", 8)\n\n\tdb := createFakeKiroDB(t, home)\n\trestore := stubSQLiteRunner(t, func(args ...string) ([]byte, error) {\n\t\tif args[0] != db {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"sqlite db = %q, want %q\", args[0], db)\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn transcriptData, nil\n\t})\n\tdefer restore()\n\n\tseedTranscriptOffset(t, repoRoot, \"conv-1\", 4)\n\n\tcachePath, err := New().ensureCachedTranscript(repoRoot, \"test-session\", \"\")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"ensureCachedTranscript() error = %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tdata, err := os.ReadFile(cachePath)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"read cached transcript: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tvar result kiroTranscript\n\tif err := json.Unmarshal(data, &result); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"parse cached transcript: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tif len(result.History) != 4 {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"history length = %d, want 4 (entries 4-7)\", len(result.History))\n\t}\n\n\tfirstPrompt := extractUserPrompt(result.History[0].User.Content)\n\tif firstPrompt != \"prompt 4\" {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"first prompt = %q, want %q\", firstPrompt, \"prompt 4\")\n\t}\n\n\toffset := readTestTranscriptOffset(t, repoRoot)\n\tif offset.Position != 8 {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"offset position = %d, want 8\", offset.Position)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestEnsureCachedTranscriptFirstCapture(t *testing.T) {\n\trepoRoot := t.TempDir()\n\thome := t.TempDir()\n\tt.Setenv(\"ENTIRE_REPO_ROOT\", repoRoot)\n\tt.Setenv(\"HOME\", home)\n\n\ttranscriptData := buildTranscript(\"conv-1\", 4)\n\n\tdb := createFakeKiroDB(t, home)\n\trestore := stubSQLiteRunner(t, func(args ...string) ([]byte, error) {\n\t\tif args[0] != db {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"sqlite db = %q, want %q\", args[0], db)\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn transcriptData, nil\n\t})\n\tdefer restore()\n\n\t// No offset file — full transcript should be written.\n\tcachePath, err := New().ensureCachedTranscript(repoRoot, \"test-session\", \"\")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"ensureCachedTranscript() error = %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tdata, err := os.ReadFile(cachePath)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"read cached transcript: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tvar result kiroTranscript\n\tif err := json.Unmarshal(data, &result); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"parse cached transcript: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tif len(result.History) != 4 {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"history length = %d, want 4 (full transcript)\", len(result.History))\n\t}\n\n\toffset := readTestTranscriptOffset(t, repoRoot)\n\tif offset.ConversationID != \"conv-1\" || offset.Position != 4 {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"offset = %+v, want {conv-1, 4}\", offset)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestEnsureCachedTranscriptConversationIDChange(t *testing.T) {\n\trepoRoot := t.TempDir()\n\thome := t.TempDir()\n\tt.Setenv(\"ENTIRE_REPO_ROOT\", repoRoot)\n\tt.Setenv(\"HOME\", home)\n\n\ttranscriptData := buildTranscript(\"new-conv\", 3)\n\n\tdb := createFakeKiroDB(t, home)\n\trestore := stubSQLiteRunner(t, func(args ...string) ([]byte, error) {\n\t\tif args[0] != db {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"sqlite db = %q, want %q\", args[0], db)\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn transcriptData, nil\n\t})\n\tdefer restore()\n\n\t// Offset from a different conversation.\n\tseedTranscriptOffset(t, repoRoot, \"old-conv\", 5)\n\n\tcachePath, err := New().ensureCachedTranscript(repoRoot, \"test-session\", \"\")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"ensureCachedTranscript() error = %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tdata, err := os.ReadFile(cachePath)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"read cached transcript: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tvar result kiroTranscript\n\tif err := json.Unmarshal(data, &result); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"parse cached transcript: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Full transcript (no trimming) since conversation_id changed.\n\tif len(result.History) != 3 {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"history length = %d, want 3 (full, new conversation)\", len(result.History))\n\t}\n\n\toffset := readTestTranscriptOffset(t, repoRoot)\n\tif offset.ConversationID != \"new-conv\" || offset.Position != 3 {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"offset = %+v, want {new-conv, 3}\", offset)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestEnsureCachedTranscriptOffsetExceedsLength(t *testing.T) {\n\trepoRoot := t.TempDir()\n\thome := t.TempDir()\n\tt.Setenv(\"ENTIRE_REPO_ROOT\", repoRoot)\n\tt.Setenv(\"HOME\", home)\n\n\ttranscriptData := buildTranscript(\"conv-1\", 3)\n\n\tdb := createFakeKiroDB(t, home)\n\trestore := stubSQLiteRunner(t, func(args ...string) ([]byte, error) {\n\t\tif args[0] != db {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"sqlite db = %q, want %q\", args[0], db)\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn transcriptData, nil\n\t})\n\tdefer restore()\n\n\t// Offset exceeds transcript length.\n\tseedTranscriptOffset(t, repoRoot, \"conv-1\", 100)\n\n\tcachePath, err := New().ensureCachedTranscript(repoRoot, \"test-session\", \"\")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"ensureCachedTranscript() error = %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tdata, err := os.ReadFile(cachePath)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"read cached transcript: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tvar result kiroTranscript\n\tif err := json.Unmarshal(data, &result); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"parse cached transcript: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Full transcript (no trimming) since offset exceeds length.\n\tif len(result.History) != 3 {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"history length = %d, want 3 (full, offset exceeded)\", len(result.History))\n\t}\n\n\toffset := readTestTranscriptOffset(t, repoRoot)\n\tif offset.Position != 3 {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"offset position = %d, want 3 (reset)\", offset.Position)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestEnsureCachedTranscriptQueriesByConversationID(t *testing.T) {\n\trepoRoot := t.TempDir()\n\thome := t.TempDir()\n\tt.Setenv(\"ENTIRE_REPO_ROOT\", repoRoot)\n\tt.Setenv(\"HOME\", home)\n\n\tdb := createFakeKiroDB(t, home)\n\tvar capturedQuery string\n\trestore := stubSQLiteRunner(t, func(args ...string) ([]byte, error) {\n\t\tif args[0] != db {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"sqlite db = %q, want %q\", args[0], db)\n\t\t}\n\t\tcapturedQuery = args[1]\n\t\treturn []byte(`{\"conversation_id\":\"target-conv\",\"history\":[]}`), nil\n\t})\n\tdefer restore()\n\n\t_, err := New().ensureCachedTranscript(repoRoot, \"test-session\", \"target-conv\")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"ensureCachedTranscript() error = %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tif !strings.Contains(capturedQuery, \"target-conv\") {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"query should contain conversation_id, got: %s\", capturedQuery)\n\t}\n\tif !strings.Contains(capturedQuery, \"json_extract\") {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"query should use json_extract for conversation_id, got: %s\", capturedQuery)\n\t}\n}"

> AGENT

All clean. Here's a summary of the final changes:

**The fix**: Replaced the broken timestamp filtering with offset-based trimming.

**4 files changed:**

1. **hooks.go** (+11/-11): Removed session start time caching (broken due to hook ordering). Kept the conversation_id query in the stop hook.

2. **transcript.go** (+103/-17): 
   - Removed `filterTranscriptByStartTime` 
   - Added `transcriptOffset` struct + `readTranscriptOffset`/`writeTranscriptOffset` helpers
   - Added `trimTranscriptHistory`: stores `{conversation_id, position}` after each capture; on next capture in the same conversation, trims `history[position:]`; resets when conversation_id changes

3. **transcript_test.go** (+280/-16): Replaced 5 timestamp tests with 5 offset tests covering: trim with offset, first capture, conversation_id change, offset exceeds length, conversation_id query

4. **e2e/lifecycle_test.go** (+4): Added `ValidateCheckpointDeep` to `TestLifecycle_SinglePromptManualCommit` — validates `full.jsonl` exists, has content, and content hash matches

```json
{
  "command": "git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfix(kiro): trim cumulative transcript to only include new entries per checkpoint\n\nKiro stores one conversation per CLI session, appending all prompt/response\ncycles to the same history array. When multiple checkpoints occur within a\nsingle session, each checkpoint was storing the full cumulative transcript\ninstead of just the new entries.\n\nFix by tracking {conversation_id, position} after each transcript capture.\nOn subsequent captures within the same conversation, trim history[position:]\nto only include new entries. Resets automatically when conversation_id\nchanges (new kiro session).\n\nAlso queries by conversation_id instead of CWD for more precise DB targeting,\nand adds ValidateCheckpointDeep to the e2e lifecycle test to catch empty or\nmissing transcripts.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.6 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Create commit"
}
```

   215→
   216→func (a *Agent) ensureIDETranscript(cwd string, sessionID string) (string, error) {
   217→	sessionsDir, err := ideWorkspaceSessionsDir(cwd)
   218→	if err != nil {
   219→		return "", err
   220→	}
   221→
   222→	indexPath := filepath.Join(sessionsDir, "sessions.json")
   223→	indexData, err := os.ReadFile(indexPath)
   224→	if err != nil {
   225→		return "", fmt.Errorf("IDE sessions.json not found: %w", err)
   226→	}
   227→
   228→	var sessions []ideSessionIndexEntry
   229→	if err := json.Unmarshal(indexData, &sessions); err != nil {
   230→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to parse IDE sessions.json: %w", err)
   231→	}
   232→	if len(sessions) == 0 {
   233→		return "", errors.New("no IDE sessions found")
   234→	}
   235→
   236→	sort.Slice(sessions, func(i, j int) bool {
   237→		return sessions[i].DateCreated > sessions[j].DateCreated
   238→	})
   239→
   240→	ideSessionID := sessions[0].SessionID
   241→	if !filepath.IsLocal(ideSessionID + ".json") {
   242→		return "", fmt.Errorf("invalid session ID: %q", ideSessionID)
   243→	}
   244→
   245→	transcriptPath := filepath.Join(sessionsDir, ideSessionID+".json")
   246→	transcriptData, err := os.ReadFile(transcriptPath)
   247→	if err != nil {
   248→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to read IDE transcript %s: %w", transcriptPath, err)
   249→	}
   250→
   251→	cachePath, err := a.cacheTranscriptPath(cwd, sessionID)
   252→	if err != nil {
   253→		return "", err
   254→	}
   255→	if err := os.WriteFile(cachePath, transcriptData, 0o600); err != nil {
   256→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to write cached IDE transcript: %w", err)
   257→	}
   258→	return cachePath, nil
   259→}
   260→
   261→func (a *Agent) captureTranscriptForStop(cwd string, sessionID string, conversationID string) string {
   262→	if sessionRef, err := a.ensureCachedTranscript(cwd, sessionID, conversationID); err == nil && sessionRef != "" {
   263→		return sessionRef
   264→	}
   265→	if sessionRef, err := a.ensureIDETranscript(cwd, sessionID); err == nil && sessionRef != "" {
   266→		return sessionRef
   267→	}
   268→	return a.createPlaceholderTranscript(cwd, sessionID)
   269→}
   270→
   271→func (a *Agent) createPlaceholderTranscript(cwd string, sessionID string) string {
   272→	cachePath, err := a.cacheTranscriptPath(cwd, sessionID)
   273→	if err != nil {
   274→		return ""
   275→	}

   277→		return ""

> AGENT

Found the root cause. Conversation `8212331e` is a **CLI** session about JavaScript — it has NO "ghost python" prompt. The ghost python was done through the **IDE**, but:

1. `querySessionID` returns the latest CLI conversation ID (`8212331e`)
2. `ensureCachedTranscript` queries the CLI DB by that ID, gets the JavaScript conversation (7 entries)
3. Offset is `{8212331e, 7}` from a previous capture — trim produces **empty** history
4. Returns "success" with an empty transcript — `ensureIDETranscript` **never gets called**

Two bugs:
- `ensureCachedTranscript` succeeds with empty content, blocking the IDE fallback
- `trimTranscriptHistory` updates the offset even when there's nothing new (preventing future captures)

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Why IDE transcripts were silently blocked**: `captureTranscriptForStop` tries CLI DB → IDE → placeholder. The CLI DB query succeeded (returned a valid file path) even though the content was empty — so the IDE fallback never ran. The lesson: a "successful" capture with empty data should be treated as a failure when there are fallback sources.

**Why the offset over-advances**: `trimTranscriptHistory` updates the offset unconditionally. When `trimFrom == totalLen` (no new entries), the offset still gets written. Future captures in the same conversation find nothing new, compounding the problem.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

## Approved Plan:
# Fix: IDE transcripts blocked by empty CLI transcript + offset over-advancement

## Context

When the user runs kiro through the IDE, the stop hook queries the CLI SQLite DB (via `querySessionID`), which returns a CLI conversation ID. `ensureCachedTranscript` fetches that CLI conversation, offset-trims it to empty (no new entries), then returns "success" with an empty file. This blocks `ensureIDETranscript` from running.

Additionally, `trimTranscriptHistory` updates the offset even when `trimFrom == totalLen` (no new entries), which prevents future captures from finding anything new.

**Evidence**: Session `87f2f4d` ("create hello ghost python") cached transcript has `conversation_id: 8212331e` (a JavaScript CLI session) with `history: []`. The actual IDE session data is in the IDE workspace directory but was never read.

## Fix

Two changes in `agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go`:

### 1. Don't update offset when there's nothing new

In `trimTranscriptHistory`, only update the offset when `trimFrom < totalLen` (there are actually new entries to return):

```go
// Only update offset when we have new entries
if trimFrom < totalLen {
    _ = writeTranscriptOffset(offsetPath, transcriptOffset{
        ConversationID: parsed.ConversationID,
        Position:       totalLen,
    })
}
```

### 2. Fail on empty transcript so IDE fallback runs

In `ensureCachedTranscript`, after writing the data, check if the transcript has any history. If not, return an error so `captureTranscriptForStop` falls through to `ensureIDETranscript`:

```go
if err := os.WriteFile(cachePath, data, 0o600); err != nil {
    return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to write cached transcript: %w", err)
}

// If the transcript has no history (e.g., offset trimmed everything),
// return error so captureTranscriptForStop can try other sources (IDE).
if parsed, err := parseTranscript(data); err == nil && len(parsed.History) == 0 {
    os.Remove(cachePath)
    return "", errors.New("CLI transcript has no history entries")
}

return cachePath, nil
```

### 3. Update tests

**Update `TestEnsureCachedTranscriptTrimsWithOffset`** — no change needed (trimFrom < totalLen case)

**Add `TestEnsureCachedTranscriptNoNewEntriesReturnsError`**:
- Seed offset: `{conversation_id: "conv-1", position: 4}`
- Stub returns 4-entry transcript with `conversation_id: "conv-1"` (same position)
- Assert `ensureCachedTranscript` returns an error
- Assert offset file is NOT updated (still position 4)

**Add `TestCaptureTranscriptForStopFallsToIDE`** (or update existing stop hook test):
- Stub CLI DB to return a transcript where offset matches total entries
- Seed IDE workspace sessions with valid transcript
- Assert the captured transcript comes from the IDE path, not the CLI

### 4. Add `ValidateCheckpointDeep` to more e2e tests

Add to `TestLifecycle_MultiplePromptsManualCommit` to catch the multi-prompt case where this bug manifests.

## Verification

1. Unit tests: `cd agents/entire-agent-kiro && go test ./internal/kiro/ -v`
2. E2e test: `E2E_AGENT=kiro go test -tags=e2e -run TestLifecycle_SinglePromptManualCommit -timeout 3m ./e2e/...`

```

> AGENT

All passing. Here's a summary of the changes:

**transcript.go** (2 fixes):
1. `trimTranscriptHistory`: Only update offset when `trimFrom < totalLen` — prevents advancing the offset when there's nothing new to capture
2. `ensureCachedTranscript`: After trimming, check if transcript has empty history. If so, return error instead of writing an empty file — allows `captureTranscriptForStop` to fall through to `ensureIDETranscript`

**transcript_test.go**:
- Updated 3 existing tests that used empty `history:[]` stubs (now rejected by empty-history check)
- Added `TestEnsureCachedTranscriptNoNewEntriesReturnsError` — verifies the error return and that the offset doesn't advance

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The logs show a clear pattern across ALL recent checkpoints: `"finalize: no transcript path, skipping"` — the Entire CLI can't find a transcript for the session being finalized. This means our offset trimming is consistently producing empty transcripts, blocking the whole checkpoint flow.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

> AGENT

Now I see the full picture. The checkpoint `bf081fd82b69` has 11 sessions but the "delete files" prompt is completely missing — it was trimmed away. The offset approach is fundamentally broken for the Entire CLI's multi-session model.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Why offset trimming can't work here**: The Entire CLI creates a SEPARATE session for each prompt cycle (agent-spawn → stop). Each session captures its own stop-hook transcript. The offset advances after each capture, so by the time the user commits and the checkpoint is created, the offset has already advanced past the interesting prompts. The CLI's design expects the **full cumulative transcript** in each session's `full.jsonl` and uses `GetTranscriptPosition` + offset-based extraction to determine what's new.

**The original "bug" was actually by design**: Storing cumulative transcripts in each session's `full.jsonl` is how the CLI works — it's not a defect. The prompt.txt and metadata correctly showed only the new prompts because the CLI applied offsets during extraction.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

The offset trimming needs to be removed entirely. I'll keep the good changes (conversation_id query, empty-history fallback for IDE, e2e test) and revert the trimming.

   130→
   131→	cachePath, err := a.cacheTranscriptPath(cwd, sessionID)
   132→	if err != nil {
   133→		return "", err
   134→	}
   135→
   136→	data := []byte(raw)
   137→	if filtered, ok := a.trimTranscriptHistory([]byte(raw)); ok {
   138→		data = filtered
   139→	}
   140→
   141→	// If the transcript has no history (e.g., offset trimmed everything),
   142→	// return error so captureTranscriptForStop can try other sources (IDE).
   143→	if parsed, parseErr := parseTranscript(data); parseErr == nil && len(parsed.History) == 0 {
   144→		return "", errors.New("CLI transcript has no history entries")
   145→	}
   146→
   147→	if err := os.WriteFile(cachePath, data, 0o600); err != nil {
   148→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to write cached transcript: %w", err)
   149→	}
   150→	return cachePath, nil
   151→}
   152→
   153→type transcriptOffset struct {
   154→	ConversationID string `json:"conversation_id"`
   155→	Position       int    `json:"position"`
   156→}
   157→
   158→func (a *Agent) transcriptOffsetPath() string {
   159→	return filepath.Join(protocol.RepoRoot(), ".entire", "tmp", "kiro-transcript-offset.json")
   160→}
   161→
   162→func readTranscriptOffset(path string) transcriptOffset {
   163→	data, err := os.ReadFile(path)
   164→	if err != nil {
   165→		return transcriptOffset{}
   166→	}
   167→	var offset transcriptOffset
   168→	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &offset); err != nil {
   169→		return transcriptOffset{}
   170→	}
   171→	return offset
   172→}
   173→
   174→func writeTranscriptOffset(path string, offset transcriptOffset) error {
   175→	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(path), 0o700); err != nil {
   176→		return err
   177→	}
   178→	data, err := json.Marshal(offset)
   179→	if err != nil {
   180→		return err
   181→	}
   182→	return os.WriteFile(path, data, 0o600)
   183→}
   184→
   185→// trimTranscriptHistory trims already-checkpointed entries from a cumulative
   186→// kiro transcript using a stored offset. Returns the re-serialized trimmed
   187→// JSON and true if trimming was applied, or (nil, false) if the full
   188→// transcript should be used as-is.
   189→func (a *Agent) trimTranscriptHistory(raw []byte) ([]byte, bool) {
   190→	parsed, err := parseTranscript(raw)
   191→	if err != nil || len(parsed.History) == 0 {
   192→		return nil, false
   193→	}
   194→
   195→	offsetPath := a.transcriptOffsetPath()
   196→	prev := readTranscriptOffset(offsetPath)
   197→	totalLen := len(parsed.History)
   198→
   199→	trimFrom := 0
   200→	if prev.ConversationID == parsed.ConversationID && prev.Position > 0 && prev.Position <= totalLen {
   201→		trimFrom = prev.Position
   202→	}
   203→
   204→	// Only update offset when there are new entries to capture.
   205→	if trimFrom < totalLen {
   206→		_ = writeTranscriptOffset(offsetPath, transcriptOffset{
   207→			ConversationID: parsed.ConversationID,
   208→			Position:       totalLen,
   209→		})
   210→	}
   211→

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Root cause found!** The Kiro IDE uses **URL-safe base64** encoding (`_` instead of `/`, `-` instead of `+`, `?` padding) for directory names. But our code in `ideWorkspaceSessionsDir` uses **standard base64** (`base64.StdEncoding` → `=` padding, `/` and `+`). The encoded paths don't match:
- Our code: `L1VzZXJzL...2tpcm9jbGk=` (standard, `=` padding)
- IDE actual: `L1VzZXJzL...2tpcm9jbGk_` (URL-safe, `?` padding)

This is why `ensureIDETranscript` always fails — it looks in the wrong directory.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

> TOOL

  "description": "Compare all base64 variants"
}

## Approved Plan:
# Fix: IDE transcripts not found due to base64 encoding mismatch

## Context

The Kiro IDE uses a **modified base64** encoding for workspace session directory names: standard base64 with `=` padding replaced by `_`. Our code uses `base64.StdEncoding` which produces `=` padding. The encoded paths don't match, so `ensureIDETranscript` always looks in the wrong directory and fails.

**Evidence**: For `/Users/alisha/Projects/test-repos/kirocli`:
- Our code produces: `...2tpcm9jbGk=`
- IDE actual dir:    `...2tpcm9jbGk_`
- Mismatch: trailing `=` vs `_`

When the CLI DB offset trimming produces empty history (correct behavior for repeated prompts in the same conversation), `ensureCachedTranscript` returns an error and `captureTranscriptForStop` falls through to `ensureIDETranscript`. But `ensureIDETranscript` can't find the directory because of the encoding mismatch — so it gets a placeholder `{}` instead of the real IDE transcript.

The "delete files" prompt exists in the IDE workspace session file `75f5e022-e0f9-4ef0-85e3-03dbb531d2ed.json` but was never read.

## Fix

One-line fix in `ideWorkspaceSessionsDir` + update test helper.

### Files to Modify

1. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go:332`** — Fix base64 encoding
2. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript_test.go:404`** — Fix test helper encoding

### Step 1: Fix encoding in `ideWorkspaceSessionsDir` (transcript.go:332)

Change:
```go
encoded := base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString([]byte(cwd))
```
To:
```go
encoded := strings.ReplaceAll(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString([]byte(cwd)), "=", "_")
```

### Step 2: Fix test helper `createIDEWorkspaceSessionsDir` (transcript_test.go:404)

Same change:
```go
encoded := strings.ReplaceAll(base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString([]byte(cwd)), "=", "_")
```

### Step 3: Add failing test using real IDE data

**`TestEnsureIDETranscriptWithModifiedBase64Encoding`**:
- Create IDE workspace sessions dir using the CORRECT encoding (`=` → `_`)
- Write a sessions.json index pointing to a transcript file
- Write an IDE-format transcript with real-ish content:
  ```json
  {"history":[
    {"message":{"role":"user","content":"please delete all the hello files"}},
    {"message":{"role":"assistant","content":"On it."}}
  ]}
  ```
- Call `ensureIDETranscript(cwd, sessionID)`
- Assert it succeeds and the cached file contains the IDE transcript

This test should FAIL before the fix (wrong directory path) and PASS after.

**`TestCaptureTranscriptFallsToIDEWhenCLIEmpty`**:
- Stub CLI DB to return a conversation where offset == total entries (empty after trim)
- Seed offset file to match
- Set up IDE workspace sessions with real transcript
- Call `captureTranscriptForStop`
- Assert the result points to a file with actual IDE transcript content (not placeholder `{}`)

### Step 4: Verify existing IDE tests still pass

The existing tests (`TestEnsureIDETranscriptCopiesLatestWorkspaceSession`, `TestParseHookStopFallsBackToIDETranscript`) use the test helper which will also be updated, so they should continue to pass.

## Execution Order (TDD)

1. Write the two new tests (Step 3) — they use the CORRECT encoding manually
2. Run tests — verify the new tests FAIL (encoding mismatch in production code)
3. Apply the one-line fix (Step 1) and test helper fix (Step 2)
4. Run tests — verify all pass
5. Run e2e test

> AGENT

All green.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**The one-character encoding bug**: The Kiro IDE uses a non-standard base64 variant where `=` padding is replaced with `_`. This is a common pattern in web/filesystem contexts where `=` is problematic (URL parameters, filenames on some systems). Our code used Go's `base64.StdEncoding` which produces standard `=` padding, so the workspace session directory path never matched.

**Why this was hidden**: The existing test helper `createIDEWorkspaceSessionsDir` used the same wrong encoding as the production code. Both produced paths with `=`, so tests passed even though neither matched the real IDE directory. Writing a test with the real encoding exposed the mismatch immediately.

**The cascade**: CLI offset trimming → empty CLI transcript → fall through to IDE → IDE lookup fails (wrong encoding) → placeholder `{}` → no transcript in checkpoint. Three bugs interacting: each individually survivable, together they produced zero transcripts.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Final diff:

> AGENT

The condensation worked fine — session `c3f37802` got 494 transcript lines. But then 10 seconds later, the **finalize** step fails:

```
WARN "finalize: no transcript path, skipping" session_id=97a6e098
```

The finalize runs for session `97a6e098` (the session that triggered the commit), NOT for `c3f37802` (the one with the transcript). Session `97a6e098` has no `session_ref` — likely the "commit" prompt session where offset trimming produced empty CLI transcript and the IDE didn't have that specific session.

This is **not a frontend issue** — it's a **CLI issue**. The checkpoint data was written correctly during condensation (session 2 has the transcript), but the CLI's `HandleTurnEnd` finalize step fails because the *triggering session* (`97a6e098`, the "commit" prompt) has no transcript path. This finalize failure likely marks the sessions as incomplete, which is why the frontend shows "No sessions."

The root cause chain:
1. User says "remove files" → session `c3f37802` → IDE transcript captured correctly (encoding fix works)
2. User says "commit" → session `97a6e098` → CLI DB has no new entries (offset trimmed to empty) AND this is a CLI-internal prompt that the IDE doesn't have as a separate session → placeholder `{}`
3. Git commit happens → CLI condenses all sessions (works) → then tries to finalize with session `97a6e098`'s transcript → no transcript path → WARN → sessions not properly linked

This is a bug in the **Entire CLI's finalize logic** — it should be able to finalize even when the triggering session has no transcript, as long as other sessions in the checkpoint do have transcripts. The external agent is doing its job correctly now (the encoding fix captured the IDE transcript into session `c3f37802`).

> AGENT

I see the issue now. The cached file for `97a6e098` has IDE content (7.9KB) — so `ensureIDETranscript` DID succeed. But the CLI's finalize still says "no transcript path." This suggests the CLI doesn't properly propagate the session_ref when the transcript comes from the IDE fallback path.

The fix in the agent: **don't let `trimTranscriptHistory` produce empty results**. When `trimFrom >= totalLen` (no new entries), return the full cumulative transcript instead of empty. This keeps `ensureCachedTranscript` as the primary path (which the CLI handles correctly), avoiding the IDE fallback that doesn't get recorded properly.

> AGENT

All green. Here's the summary of the two fixes:

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Fix 1 — base64 encoding** (`ideWorkspaceSessionsDir`): The Kiro IDE uses `=` → `_` replacement in base64 directory names. One line: `strings.ReplaceAll(..., "=", "_")`. This lets the IDE fallback path find the workspace sessions.

**Fix 2 — never produce empty transcripts** (`trimTranscriptHistory`): When `trimFrom >= totalLen` (no new entries since last capture), return the full cumulative transcript instead of empty. This ensures `ensureCachedTranscript` always returns a valid session_ref that the CLI can use for finalization. The cumulative data is correct — the CLI's own offset extraction handles deduplication.

**Why both fixes are needed**: The encoding fix lets the IDE path work. The empty-transcript fix ensures the CLI path always provides a valid session_ref, which the CLI needs for its finalize step. Without it, even when the IDE path succeeds, the CLI doesn't propagate the session_ref properly (a CLI-side issue worked around from the agent).
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Ready to commit and push when you say so.

## Complete Transcript Bug Investigation

I've conducted a thorough investigation of the Kiro IDE transcript bug. Here's the complete picture:

### The Bug

When user types "add python hello world - ide" in Kiro IDE, the checkpoint shows "craete hello ghost golang" instead — a completely different prompt from a separate CLI conversation.

### Root Cause

The problem is in the **priority order** of transcript sources in `captureTranscriptForStop()` at `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go:272-280`.

**Current (broken) order:**
1. Try `ensureCachedTranscript()` — queries the **CLI DB** first ❌
2. Try `ensureIDETranscript()` — queries IDE sessions second (never reached)
3. Use placeholder third

**What happens:**

1. **hooks.go:84** calls `querySessionID(cwd)`, which queries the CLI DB for the LATEST conversation by `updated_at DESC`
   - Returns: `de956a51-ab64-4023-92c3-c93cd67be459` (the wrong CLI conversation)

2. **transcript.go:273** tries `ensureCachedTranscript()` with that conversation_id
   - Queries CLI DB: finds the conversation with prompt "craete hello ghost golnag"
   - Calls `trimTranscriptHistory()` which returns the full transcript (6 entries, position=6)
   - **Returns success** with WRONG data

3. **transcript.go:276** tries `ensureIDETranscript()`
   - **Never executed** because step 2 succeeded
   - Would have found the correct IDE session: `6930c75e-066b-48a4-94c0-f657ce198188` with "add python hello world - ide"

### Data Flow Evidence

**IDE Session (Correct):**
- Location: `~/Library/Application Support/Kiro/User/globalStorage/kiro.kiroagent/workspace-sessions/.../6930c75e-066b-48a4-94c0-f657ce198188.json`
- dateCreated: 1774112778524 (most recent)
- First prompt: "add python hello world - ide"
- Exists and is ready to be used

**CLI DB (Wrong):**
- Latest conversation (by updated_at DESC): `de956a51-ab64-4023-92c3-c93cd67be459`
- First prompt: "craete hello ghost golnag"
- Completely unrelated to the current IDE session

**Offset File:**
- Contains: `{"conversation_id": "de956a51-ab64-4023-92c3-c93cd67be459", "position": 6}`
- Stored from a PREVIOUS checkpoint
- Gets matched incorrectly by the CLI query

### Key Insight

**The stop hook is IDE-only.** The file `.kiro/hooks/entire-stop.kiro.hook` is registered for the IDE's `agentStop` event. The CLI has its own finalization and doesn't use this hook.

Therefore:
- Stop hook input always comes from IDE
- IDE resources should be tried FIRST
- CLI DB should be a FALLBACK only

### The Solution

**Reverse the priority order in `captureTranscriptForStop()`:**

```go
// BEFORE (WRONG):
if sessionRef, err := a.ensureCachedTranscript(cwd, sessionID, conversationID); err == nil && sessionRef != "" {
  return sessionRef  // CLI tried first — WRONG!
}
if sessionRef, err := a.ensureIDETranscript(cwd, sessionID); err == nil && sessionRef != "" {
  return sessionRef  // IDE tried second — never reached
}

// AFTER (CORRECT):
if sessionRef, err := a.ensureIDETranscript(cwd, sessionID); err == nil && sessionRef != "" {
  return sessionRef  // IDE tried first — CORRECT!
}
if sessionRef, err := a.ensureCachedTranscript(cwd, sessionID, conversationID); err == nil && sessionRef != "" {
  return sessionRef  // CLI tried second — fallback
}
```

### Why This Fix Is Safe

1. **IDE sessions exist in IDE context** → `ensureIDETranscript()` succeeds
2. **Backwards compatible** → If no IDE sessions exist, falls back to CLI DB
3. **No new dependencies** → Just reorders existing code paths
4. **All tests pass** → Both paths already tested and working
5. **Edge cases covered:**
   - IDE + CLI both exist → Uses IDE ✓
   - Only IDE exists → Uses IDE ✓
   - Only CLI exists → Uses CLI ✓
   - Neither exists → Uses placeholder ✓

### Test Changes Required

The test `TestParseHookStopPrefersSQLiteTranscript` (line 255) tests the OLD (wrong) behavior where CLI is preferred. With the fix:
- Should be renamed to `TestParseHookStopUsesIDEWhenAvailable`
- Should verify IDE transcript is used when both exist
- The existing fallback test `TestParseHookStopFallsBackToIDETranscript` (line 312) becomes the new fallback scenario

### Optional Additional Fix

**hooks.go:84** calls `querySessionID(cwd)` but this becomes unnecessary with IDE-first logic:
```go
conversationID, _ := a.querySessionID(cwd)  // No longer needed
```

This can be removed to simplify the code and avoid unnecessary CLI DB queries.

## Approved Plan:
# Fix: IDE sessions get wrong transcript from CLI DB

## Context

When the user runs kiro through the IDE, the stop hook queries the CLI SQLite DB first (`ensureCachedTranscript`), which returns a completely unrelated CLI conversation. Since this succeeds, `ensureIDETranscript` is never reached — even though the correct IDE transcript exists in the workspace sessions directory.

**Evidence**: User typed "add python hello world - ide" in the IDE. Checkpoint shows "craete hello ghost golang" — a prompt from a different CLI conversation returned by `querySessionID` (latest by `updated_at`).

The CLI path works fine for CLI sessions. The fix must support both.

## Fix

Reverse the priority in `captureTranscriptForStop` — try IDE first, CLI second.

### File: `agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go`

**Line ~272**, change:
```go
func (a *Agent) captureTranscriptForStop(cwd string, sessionID string, conversationID string) string {
	if sessionRef, err := a.ensureCachedTranscript(cwd, sessionID, conversationID); err == nil && sessionRef != "" {
		return sessionRef
	}
	if sessionRef, err := a.ensureIDETranscript(cwd, sessionID); err == nil && sessionRef != "" {
		return sessionRef
	}
	return a.createPlaceholderTranscript(cwd, sessionID)
}
```
To:
```go
func (a *Agent) captureTranscriptForStop(cwd string, sessionID string, conversationID string) string {
	if sessionRef, err := a.ensureIDETranscript(cwd, sessionID); err == nil && sessionRef != "" {
		return sessionRef
	}
	if sessionRef, err := a.ensureCachedTranscript(cwd, sessionID, conversationID); err == nil && sessionRef != "" {
		return sessionRef
	}
	return a.createPlaceholderTranscript(cwd, sessionID)
}
```

**Why this is safe for both:**
- **IDE sessions**: `ensureIDETranscript` finds the workspace session → returns correct transcript
- **CLI sessions**: No IDE workspace sessions exist → `ensureIDETranscript` fails → falls to `ensureCachedTranscript` → works as before

### Tests

**Update `TestParseHookStopPrefersSQLiteTranscript`** — this test has both CLI DB and no IDE sessions set up. With IDE-first ordering, CLI DB should still be used as fallback. The test logic stays the same, just rename to `TestParseHookStopFallsBackToCLIWhenNoIDE`.

**Add `TestCaptureTranscriptPrefersIDEOverCLI`**:
- Set up BOTH CLI DB (with wrong conversation) AND IDE workspace sessions (with correct transcript)
- Assert IDE transcript is used, not CLI

## Execution (TDD)

1. Write `TestCaptureTranscriptPrefersIDEOverCLI` — should FAIL (CLI path wins)
2. Swap the order in `captureTranscriptForStop`
3. Run tests — all should pass
4. Run e2e test

## Verification

1. `cd agents/entire-agent-kiro && go test ./internal/kiro/ -v`
2. `cd e2e && E2E_AGENT=kiro go test -tags=e2e -run TestLifecycle_SinglePromptManualCommit -timeout 3m ./...`

```

> AGENT

All clean. The fix is a 3-line swap in `captureTranscriptForStop` — IDE tried first, CLI as fallback. Both paths work for their respective use cases.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Why IDE-first is correct**: The stop hook is registered in `.kiro/hooks/entire-stop.kiro.hook` for the IDE's `agentStop` event. When the IDE fires this hook, the IDE workspace session is the authoritative transcript source. The CLI DB is a separate data store that may contain unrelated conversations. For CLI-only sessions (no IDE), the IDE path fails fast (no workspace-sessions directory) and the CLI DB fallback handles it.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

   228→	sessionsDir, err := ideWorkspaceSessionsDir(cwd)
   229→	if err != nil {

   231→	}

   237→	}

   241→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to parse IDE sessions.json: %w", err)

   249→	})

   265→	}
   266→	if err := os.WriteFile(cachePath, transcriptData, 0o600); err != nil {
   267→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to write cached IDE transcript: %w", err)

> AGENT

Now it picks the correct IDE session — "add python hello world - ide" instead of "create hello world rust".

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**The three-part fix for IDE transcripts**:
1. **Base64 encoding** (`=` → `_`): Find the IDE workspace sessions directory
2. **IDE-first priority**: Try IDE transcript before CLI DB
3. **Prompt matching**: Cache the prompt from `user-prompt-submit`, use it to match the correct IDE session by title when multiple sessions exist

Without prompt matching, `ensureIDETranscript` always picks the latest session by `dateCreated` — which is wrong when the user has opened a newer session before the current one's stop hook fires.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Since this succeeds, `ensureIDETranscript` is never reached — even though the correct IDE transcript exists in the workspace sessions directory.

Now I have a clear picture. Let me create a comprehensive report with my findings:

## Summary of IDE Transcript Missing Tool Calls Issue

I've thoroughly investigated the IDE transcript handling in the kiro agent. Here are my findings:

### Key Problem: `ensureIDETranscript` Copies Raw IDE Format Without Conversion

**File:** `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go`

**Lines 228-271 (ensureIDETranscript function):**
```go
func (a *Agent) ensureIDETranscript(cwd string, sessionID string) (string, error) {
	// ... finds IDE transcript ...
	transcriptData, err := os.ReadFile(transcriptPath)
	if err != nil {
		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to read IDE transcript %s: %w", transcriptPath, err)
	}

	cachePath, err := a.cacheTranscriptPath(cwd, sessionID)
	if err != nil {
		return "", err
	}
	if err := os.WriteFile(cachePath, transcriptData, 0o600); err != nil {  // <-- BUG HERE
		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to write cached IDE transcript: %w", err)
	}
	return cachePath, nil
}
```

**The problem:** The function reads the IDE transcript file and writes it **as-is** to the cache without any conversion. This means the cached transcript remains in IDE format.

### IDE Format vs CLI Format - Structural Differences

**IDE Format (from test repo examples):**
```json
{
  "history": [
    {
      "message": {
        "role": "user",
        "content": [{"type": "text", "text": "create hello world python"}]
      }
    },
    {
      "message": {
        "role": "assistant",
        "content": "On it."
      }
    }
  ]
}
```

**CLI Format (from `/Users/alisha/Projects/test-repos/kirocli/.entire/tmp/038eda0f297b947e1f117aa7968fa670.json`):**
```json
{
  "conversation_id": "de956a51-ab64-4023-92c3-c93cd67be459",
  "history": [
    {
      "user": {
        "content": {"Prompt": {"prompt": "craete hello ghost golnag"}},
        "timestamp": "2026-03-21T00:12:47.772650-07:00"
      },
      "assistant": {
        "ToolUse": {
          "message_id": "af6fb74e-469e-4c7e-908d-811b160d4aa1",
          "tool_uses": [
            {
              "id": "REDACTED",
              "name": "fs_write",
              "args": {"command": "create", "path": "/....", "file_text": "..."}
            }
          ]
        }
      }
    }
  ]
}
```

Key differences:
1. **IDE**: `history[i].message.role`, `history[i].message.content` (assistant messages are plain strings or content blocks)
2. **CLI**: `history[i].user.content` and `history[i].assistant` (assistant contains `ToolUse` or `Response` wrapper objects)
3. **IDE tool calls**: Stored in message content as text/blocks, NOT in a structured `ToolUse` object
4. **CLI tool calls**: Structured as `ToolUse` with `tool_uses[]` array containing tool name, id, and args

### How Tool Calls Get Lost

**Extraction function (lines 572-609):**
```go
func extractModifiedFilesFromHistory(entries []kiroHistoryEntry) []string {
	seen := make(map[string]bool)
	var files []string

	for i := range entries {
		for _, path := range extractFilesFromAssistant(entries[i].Assistant) {  // <-- Looks for CLI format
			if path == "" || seen[path] {
				continue
			}
			seen[path] = true
			files = append(files, path)
		}
	}
	return files
}

func extractFilesFromAssistant(raw json.RawMessage) []string {
	if len(raw) == 0 {
		return nil
	}

	var toolUseContent kiroToolUseContent  // <-- Expects CLI format ToolUse wrapper
	if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &toolUseContent); err != nil || len(toolUseContent.ToolUse.ToolUses) == 0 {
		return nil
	}
	// ... extract file paths from tool_uses array ...
}
```

**The problem:** When an IDE transcript is cached as-is (without conversion):
1. The IDE format `message.content` (string or blocks) is NOT converted to CLI format `ToolUse` object
2. When `extractModifiedFilesFromHistory` tries to parse `entries[i].Assistant`, it expects `{"ToolUse": {"tool_uses": [...]}}` (CLI format)
3. Instead, it finds plain text or block arrays (IDE format), so unmarshaling fails and returns empty
4. **Result:** Tool calls are invisible to the extraction logic

### What SHOULD Happen

There's already a conversion function that handles this! (Lines 447-481)

```go
func tryParseIDETranscript(data []byte) *kiroTranscript {
	var ide kiroIDETranscript
	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &ide); err != nil {
		return nil
	}
	if len(ide.History) == 0 || ide.History[0].Message.Role == "" {
		return nil
	}
	return convertIDETranscript(&ide)  // <-- Converts to CLI format
}

func convertIDETranscript(ide *kiroIDETranscript) *kiroTranscript {
	// Pairs user/assistant messages and wraps them in CLI format structures
	// But only extracts text, not tool calls!
}
```

### The Core Issue: IDE Transcripts Don't Store Tool Calls in a Structured Way

Looking at the IDE transcript format, assistant messages just contain plain text strings or content blocks with text. The IDE agent doesn't store tool calls in the same `ToolUse` structured format that the CLI does. When the converter runs, it only extracts the text content and ignores any tool information.

**The question becomes:** Does the IDE even store tool call information in its transcript files, or is that metadata stored elsewhere?

### Current Workaround Path

**Lines 420-445 (parseTranscript function):**
```go
func parseTranscript(data []byte) (*kiroTranscript, error) {
	// ... unmarshals as CLI format ...
	if isCLITranscript(&transcript) {
		return &transcript, nil
	}

	// If not CLI format, tries to parse as IDE format and convert
	if converted := tryParseIDETranscript(data); converted != nil {
		return converted, nil
	}

	return &transcript, nil
}
```

This conversion happens **at parse time**, but only for text content. The problem is that:
1. When `ensureIDETranscript` caches the IDE transcript, it saves it in IDE format
2. Later when parsed via `parseTranscript`, it gets converted to CLI format
3. **But the conversion only extracts text, not tool calls** because the IDE format doesn't preserve tool metadata in a parseable way

### Test Evidence

**File:** `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript_test.go`

- **Lines 186-228:** `TestEnsureIDETranscriptCopiesLatestWorkspaceSession` - Tests that IDE transcript is copied as-is
- **Lines 225-226:** Assertion confirms raw IDE format is cached: `{"history":[{"message":{"role":"assistant","content":"new"}}]}`
- **Lines 43-70:** `testIDEAnalyzerTranscript` - IDE test transcript with only text content, no tool calls
- **Lines 468-486:** `TestExtractModifiedFilesFromCLITranscript` - Tests extraction from CLI format only

There are **NO tests** for extracting tool calls from IDE transcripts because IDE transcripts don't contain structured tool call data.

### Root Cause Chain

1. IDE stores agent responses as plain text strings/blocks (no structured tool metadata)
2. `ensureIDETranscript` copies IDE transcripts without converting them
3. Cached IDE transcripts remain in IDE format
4. `parseTranscript` detects and converts IDE format at parse time, but conversion only extracts text
5. `extractModifiedFilesFromHistory` expects CLI format `ToolUse` objects and finds nothing
6. **Result:** Tool calls appear to be missing

### Key Files to Review

- `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go` (lines 228-271, 447-517)
- `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/types.go` (lines 103-119 for IDE structures)
- `/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript_test.go` (test cases showing format differences)

  "content": "# Fix: IDE transcripts missing tool calls\n\n## Context\n\nThe Kiro IDE workspace session files (`~/Library/Application Support/Kiro/.../workspace-sessions/<encoded>/<sessionId>.json`) only store text summaries — assistant entries are just `\"On it.\"` with no structured tool call data (`fs_write`, `execute_bash`, etc.). This means:\n- `extractModifiedFiles` returns nothing (no `ToolUse` entries to parse)\n- Checkpoints show no steps/tools in the frontend\n- `files_touched` in checkpoint metadata is empty\n\nThe CLI DB format (`conversations_v2`) stores full tool call data, but for pure IDE repos (like `kireide`), there are zero CLI DB entries.\n\n**Evidence from `/Users/alisha/Projects/test-repos/kireide`**:\n- IDE session `8dbff19c`: 12 entries, all assistant content is `\"On it.\"` — no tool calls\n- CLI DB: zero entries for this repo\n- Checkpoint `004c9e0d32d2`: transcript has prompts but zero tool detail\n\n## Root cause\n\nThe `post-tool-use` hook receives `ToolName`, `ToolInput`, and `ToolResponse` from kiro but currently returns `nil, nil` (no-op). This tool call data is discarded. The IDE workspace format never stored it.\n\n## Fix: Capture tool calls from `post-tool-use` hooks\n\nAccumulate tool call info from `post-tool-use` hooks into a session-scoped file. In `ensureIDETranscript`, merge these captured tool calls into the IDE transcript before caching, converting it to the CLI format that `extractModifiedFiles` understands.\n\n### Files to modify\n\n1. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/hooks.go`** — Capture tool calls in `post-tool-use`\n2. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go`** — Merge tool calls into IDE transcript\n3. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript_test.go`** — Tests\n\n### Step 1: Accumulate tool calls in `post-tool-use` (hooks.go)\n\nAdd a constant `toolCallsFile = \"kiro-tool-calls.jsonl\"` (JSONL, one tool call per line).\n\nIn `ParseHook`, replace the `HookNamePostToolUse` no-op:\n\n```go\ncase HookNamePostToolUse:\n    if raw.ToolName != \"\" {\n        a.appendToolCall(raw.ToolName, raw.ToolInput)\n    }\n    return nil, nil\n```\n\nAdd helper `appendToolCall(name string, input json.RawMessage)`:\n- Appends a JSON line `{\"name\":\"fs_write\",\"args\":{...}}` to `.entire/tmp/kiro-tool-calls.jsonl`\n- Uses append mode (`os.OpenFile` with `O_APPEND|O_CREATE|O_WRONLY`)\n- Only captures file-modification tools (`fs_write`, `fs_edit`) to keep it focused\n\nAdd `readAndClearToolCalls() []kiroToolCall`:\n- Reads the JSONL file, parses each line into `kiroToolCall` struct\n- Deletes the file after reading (consumed by stop hook)\n- Returns empty slice on error\n\nClear the file in `clearCachedSessionID()` (alongside session ID cleanup).\n\n### Step 2: Merge tool calls into IDE transcript (transcript.go)\n\nIn `ensureIDETranscript`, after reading the IDE transcript data and before writing to cache:\n\n1. Call `a.readAndClearToolCalls()` to get captured tool calls\n2. If tool calls exist AND the transcript is IDE format:\n   - Parse the IDE transcript\n   - Convert to CLI format using existing `convertIDETranscript`\n   - Inject the tool calls into the appropriate assistant entries (pair them with the user entries — each user prompt's corresponding assistant entry gets the tool calls that happened between that prompt and the next)\n   - Serialize the enriched CLI-format transcript\n   - Write the enriched version to cache instead of the raw IDE data\n\nThe enriched transcript uses the existing `kiroTranscript` format with `kiroHistoryEntry` containing `kiroToolUseContent` in the `Assistant` field — the same format that `extractModifiedFilesFromHistory` already parses.\n\n**Simple approach for pairing**: Since `post-tool-use` hooks fire between `user-prompt-submit` and `stop`, ALL captured tool calls belong to the LAST user entry in the IDE transcript. Assign all tool calls to the last user-assistant pair.\n\n### Step 3: Tests\n\n**`TestPostToolUseHookCapturesToolCalls`**:\n- Fire `ParseHook(HookNamePostToolUse, ...)` with `tool_name: \"fs_write\"` and `tool_input: {\"path\": \"/repo/hello.py\"}`\n- Verify the JSONL file exists with the tool call\n\n**`TestEnsureIDETranscriptMergesToolCalls`**:\n- Seed IDE workspace sessions with a real-ish transcript (user+assistant with \"On it.\")\n- Seed the tool calls JSONL file with captured `fs_write` calls\n- Call `ensureIDETranscript`\n- Parse the cached result\n- Assert it's in CLI format with `ToolUse` entries containing the file paths\n- Assert `extractModifiedFilesFromHistory` returns the expected files\n\n**`TestEnsureIDETranscriptWithoutToolCalls`**:\n- Same as above but no JSONL file\n- Assert the IDE transcript is still cached (as-is in IDE format, same as current behavior)\n\n### Step 4: Update e2e test\n\nThe existing `ValidateCheckpointDeep` call in `TestLifecycle_SinglePromptManualCommit` already checks transcript content. Add `FilesTouched` to the validation to ensure tool calls are captured:\n\n```go\ntestutil.ValidateCheckpointDeep(t, s.Dir, testutil.DeepCheckpointValidation{\n    CheckpointID:              cpID,\n    FilesTouched:              []string{\"hello.txt\"},\n    ExpectedTranscriptContent: []string{\"hello\"},\n})\n```\n\n## Key types to reuse\n\n- `kiroToolCall` (`types.go:97`) — `{ID, Name, Args}` — already used by CLI format\n- `kiroToolUseContent` (`types.go:79`) — wrapper for assistant `ToolUse` entries\n- `kiroToolUsePayload` (`types.go:83`) — `{MessageID, ToolUses []kiroToolCall}`\n- `convertIDETranscript` (`transcript.go:458`) — existing IDE→CLI converter\n- `extractModifiedFilesFromHistory` (`transcript.go:572`) — existing extractor\n\n## Verification\n\n1. Unit tests: `cd agents/entire-agent-kiro && go test ./internal/kiro/ -v`\n2. Manual: Build binary, run `post-tool-use` hook with fs_write input, then `stop` hook. Check cached transcript has tool call data.\n3. E2e: `cd e2e && E2E_AGENT=kiro go test -tags=e2e -run TestLifecycle_SinglePromptManualCommit -timeout 3m ./...`\n"
}

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Why IDE transcripts have no tool calls**: The Kiro IDE stores a high-level conversation view — user prompts and summary responses ("On it."). The actual tool execution (`fs_write`, `execute_bash`) happens in the kiro agent backend, and those details never make it into the IDE's workspace session files. The CLI DB stores the full agent interaction, but pure IDE repos have zero CLI DB entries.

**The hook pipeline is the answer**: The `post-tool-use` hook fires after every tool call and receives `tool_name` + `tool_input` + `tool_response`. This data is currently discarded (`return nil, nil`). By accumulating these into a JSONL file and merging them into the IDE transcript at stop time, we can reconstruct the structured `ToolUse` entries that `extractModifiedFiles` needs.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

> TOOL
