## Approved Plan:
# Fix: IDE transcripts missing tool calls

## Context

The Kiro IDE workspace session files (`~/Library/Application Support/Kiro/.../workspace-sessions/<encoded>/<sessionId>.json`) only store text summaries — assistant entries are just `"On it."` with no structured tool call data (`fs_write`, `execute_bash`, etc.). This means:
- `extractModifiedFiles` returns nothing (no `ToolUse` entries to parse)
- Checkpoints show no steps/tools in the frontend
- `files_touched` in checkpoint metadata is empty

The CLI DB format (`conversations_v2`) stores full tool call data, but for pure IDE repos (like `kireide`), there are zero CLI DB entries.

**Evidence from `/Users/alisha/Projects/test-repos/kireide`**:
- IDE session `8dbff19c`: 12 entries, all assistant content is `"On it."` — no tool calls
- CLI DB: zero entries for this repo
- Checkpoint `004c9e0d32d2`: transcript has prompts but zero tool detail

## Root cause

The `post-tool-use` hook receives `ToolName`, `ToolInput`, and `ToolResponse` from kiro but currently returns `nil, nil` (no-op). This tool call data is discarded. The IDE workspace format never stored it.

## Fix: Capture tool calls from `post-tool-use` hooks

Accumulate tool call info from `post-tool-use` hooks into a session-scoped file. In `ensureIDETranscript`, merge these captured tool calls into the IDE transcript before caching, converting it to the CLI format that `extractModifiedFiles` understands.

### Files to modify

1. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/hooks.go`** — Capture tool calls in `post-tool-use`
2. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript.go`** — Merge tool calls into IDE transcript
3. **`agents/entire-agent-kiro/internal/kiro/transcript_test.go`** — Tests

### Step 1: Accumulate tool calls in `post-tool-use` (hooks.go)

Add a constant `toolCallsFile = "kiro-tool-calls.jsonl"` (JSONL, one tool call per line).

In `ParseHook`, replace the `HookNamePostToolUse` no-op:

```go
case HookNamePostToolUse:
    if raw.ToolName != "" {
        a.appendToolCall(raw.ToolName, raw.ToolInput)
    }
    return nil, nil
```

Add helper `appendToolCall(name string, input json.RawMessage)`:
- Appends a JSON line `{"name":"fs_write","args":{...}}` to `.entire/tmp/kiro-tool-calls.jsonl`
- Uses append mode (`os.OpenFile` with `O_APPEND|O_CREATE|O_WRONLY`)
- Only captures file-modification tools (`fs_write`, `fs_edit`) to keep it focused

Add `readAndClearToolCalls() []kiroToolCall`:
- Reads the JSONL file, parses each line into `kiroToolCall` struct
- Deletes the file after reading (consumed by stop hook)
- Returns empty slice on error

Clear the file in `clearCachedSessionID()` (alongside session ID cleanup).

### Step 2: Merge tool calls into IDE transcript (transcript.go)

In `ensureIDETranscript`, after reading the IDE transcript data and before writing to cache:

1. Call `a.readAndClearToolCalls()` to get captured tool calls
2. If tool calls exist AND the transcript is IDE format:
   - Parse the IDE transcript
   - Convert to CLI format using existing `convertIDETranscript`
   - Inject the tool calls into the appropriate assistant entries (pair them with the user entries — each user prompt's corresponding assistant entry gets the tool calls that happened between that prompt and the next)
   - Serialize the enriched CLI-format transcript
   - Write the enriched version to cache instead of the raw IDE data

The enriched transcript uses the existing `kiroTranscript` format with `kiroHistoryEntry` containing `kiroToolUseContent` in the `Assistant` field — the same format that `extractModifiedFilesFromHistory` already parses.

**Simple approach for pairing**: Since `post-tool-use` hooks fire between `user-prompt-submit` and `stop`, ALL captured tool calls belong to the LAST user entry in the IDE transcript. Assign all tool calls to the last user-assistant pair.

### Step 3: Tests

**`TestPostToolUseHookCapturesToolCalls`**:
- Fire `ParseHook(HookNamePostToolUse, ...)` with `tool_name: "fs_write"` and `tool_input: {"path": "/repo/hello.py"}`
- Verify the JSONL file exists with the tool call

**`TestEnsureIDETranscriptMergesToolCalls`**:
- Seed IDE workspace sessions with a real-ish transcript (user+assistant with "On it.")
- Seed the tool calls JSONL file with captured `fs_write` calls
- Call `ensureIDETranscript`
- Parse the cached result
- Assert it's in CLI format with `ToolUse` entries containing the file paths
- Assert `extractModifiedFilesFromHistory` returns the expected files

**`TestEnsureIDETranscriptWithoutToolCalls`**:
- Same as above but no JSONL file
- Assert the IDE transcript is still cached (as-is in IDE format, same as current behavior)

### Step 4: Update e2e test

The existing `ValidateCheckpointDeep` call in `TestLifecycle_SinglePromptManualCommit` already checks transcript content. Add `FilesTouched` to the validation to ensure tool calls are captured:

```go
testutil.ValidateCheckpointDeep(t, s.Dir, testutil.DeepCheckpointValidation{
    CheckpointID:              cpID,
    FilesTouched:              []string{"hello.txt"},
    ExpectedTranscriptContent: []string{"hello"},
})
```

## Key types to reuse

- `kiroToolCall` (`types.go:97`) — `{ID, Name, Args}` — already used by CLI format
- `kiroToolUseContent` (`types.go:79`) — wrapper for assistant `ToolUse` entries
- `kiroToolUsePayload` (`types.go:83`) — `{MessageID, ToolUses []kiroToolCall}`
- `convertIDETranscript` (`transcript.go:458`) — existing IDE→CLI converter
- `extractModifiedFilesFromHistory` (`transcript.go:572`) — existing extractor

## Verification

1. Unit tests: `cd agents/entire-agent-kiro && go test ./internal/kiro/ -v`
2. Manual: Build binary, run `post-tool-use` hook with fs_write input, then `stop` hook. Check cached transcript has tool call data.
3. E2e: `cd e2e && E2E_AGENT=kiro go test -tags=e2e -run TestLifecycle_SinglePromptManualCommit -timeout 3m ./...`

```

  "new_string": "func (a *Agent) clearCachedSessionID() {\n\t_ = os.Remove(a.sessionIDCachePath())\n\t_ = os.Remove(a.toolCallsPath())\n}\n\nfunc (a *Agent) appendToolCall(name string, input json.RawMessage) {\n\tcall := kiroToolCall{Name: name, Args: input}\n\tline, err := json.Marshal(call)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tpath := a.toolCallsPath()\n\tif err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(path), 0o700); err != nil {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tf, err := os.OpenFile(path, os.O_APPEND|os.O_CREATE|os.O_WRONLY, 0o600)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tdefer f.Close()\n\t_, _ = f.Write(append(line, '\\n'))\n}\n\nfunc (a *Agent) readAndClearToolCalls() []kiroToolCall {\n\tpath := a.toolCallsPath()\n\tdata, err := os.ReadFile(path)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\t_ = os.Remove(path)\n\n\tvar calls []kiroToolCall\n\tfor _, line := range strings.Split(strings.TrimSpace(string(data)), \"\\n\") {\n\t\tif line == \"\" {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tvar call kiroToolCall\n\t\tif err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(line), &call); err == nil {\n\t\t\tcalls = append(calls, call)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn calls\n}\n\nfunc (a *Agent) toolCallsPath() string {\n\treturn filepath.Join(protocol.RepoRoot(), \".entire\", \"tmp\", toolCallsFile)\n}"
}

   227→
   228→func (a *Agent) ensureIDETranscript(cwd string, sessionID string) (string, error) {
   229→	sessionsDir, err := ideWorkspaceSessionsDir(cwd)
   230→	if err != nil {
   231→		return "", err
   232→	}
   233→
   234→	indexPath := filepath.Join(sessionsDir, "sessions.json")
   235→	indexData, err := os.ReadFile(indexPath)
   236→	if err != nil {
   237→		return "", fmt.Errorf("IDE sessions.json not found: %w", err)
   238→	}
   239→
   240→	var sessions []ideSessionIndexEntry
   241→	if err := json.Unmarshal(indexData, &sessions); err != nil {
   242→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to parse IDE sessions.json: %w", err)
   243→	}
   244→	if len(sessions) == 0 {
   245→		return "", errors.New("no IDE sessions found")
   246→	}
   247→
   248→	sort.Slice(sessions, func(i, j int) bool {
   249→		return sessions[i].DateCreated > sessions[j].DateCreated
   250→	})
   251→
   252→	ideSessionID := sessions[0].SessionID
   253→	if !filepath.IsLocal(ideSessionID + ".json") {
   254→		return "", fmt.Errorf("invalid session ID: %q", ideSessionID)
   255→	}
   256→
   257→	transcriptPath := filepath.Join(sessionsDir, ideSessionID+".json")
   258→	transcriptData, err := os.ReadFile(transcriptPath)
   259→	if err != nil {
   260→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to read IDE transcript %s: %w", transcriptPath, err)
   261→	}
   262→
   263→	cachePath, err := a.cacheTranscriptPath(cwd, sessionID)
   264→	if err != nil {
   265→		return "", err
   266→	}
   267→	if err := os.WriteFile(cachePath, transcriptData, 0o600); err != nil {
   268→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to write cached IDE transcript: %w", err)
   269→	}
   270→	return cachePath, nil
   271→}

  "old_string": "\tcachePath, err := a.cacheTranscriptPath(cwd, sessionID)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn \"\", err\n\t}\n\tif err := os.WriteFile(cachePath, transcriptData, 0o600); err != nil {\n\t\treturn \"\", fmt.Errorf(\"failed to write cached IDE transcript: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn cachePath, nil\n}",
  "new_string": "\t// Merge captured tool calls (from post-tool-use hooks) into the IDE\n\t// transcript. The IDE format only stores text summaries (\"On it.\"), so\n\t// tool call details would be lost without this enrichment.\n\tdata := transcriptData\n\tif toolCalls := a.readAndClearToolCalls(); len(toolCalls) > 0 {\n\t\tif enriched := enrichIDETranscriptWithToolCalls(transcriptData, toolCalls); enriched != nil {\n\t\t\tdata = enriched\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tcachePath, err := a.cacheTranscriptPath(cwd, sessionID)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn \"\", err\n\t}\n\tif err := os.WriteFile(cachePath, data, 0o600); err != nil {\n\t\treturn \"\", fmt.Errorf(\"failed to write cached IDE transcript: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn cachePath, nil\n}\n\n// enrichIDETranscriptWithToolCalls converts an IDE transcript to CLI format\n// and injects the captured tool calls into the last assistant entry. Returns\n// the re-serialized CLI-format JSON, or nil if conversion fails.\nfunc enrichIDETranscriptWithToolCalls(ideData []byte, toolCalls []kiroToolCall) []byte {\n\tconverted := tryParseIDETranscript(ideData)\n\tif converted == nil || len(converted.History) == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\t// Assign all tool calls to the last assistant entry, since post-tool-use\n\t// hooks fire between user-prompt-submit and stop for the current turn.\n\ttoolUse := kiroToolUseContent{\n\t\tToolUse: kiroToolUsePayload{ToolUses: toolCalls},\n\t}\n\ttoolUseJSON, err := json.Marshal(toolUse)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tlastIdx := len(converted.History) - 1\n\tconverted.History[lastIdx].Assistant = toolUseJSON\n\n\tresult, err := json.Marshal(converted)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\treturn result\n}"
}

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**How tool call enrichment works**: The Kiro IDE fires `post-tool-use` hooks after every tool execution, passing `tool_name` and `tool_input`. We now accumulate these into a JSONL file (`.entire/tmp/kiro-tool-calls.jsonl`). When `ensureIDETranscript` runs at stop time, it reads these captured tool calls, converts the IDE transcript to CLI format using `convertIDETranscript`, and injects the tool calls into the last assistant entry. The result is a CLI-format transcript with `ToolUse` entries that `extractModifiedFilesFromHistory` can parse.

**Why JSONL append**: Tool calls happen sequentially during a prompt cycle. Using append mode (`O_APPEND`) means each `post-tool-use` hook invocation adds one line without reading/parsing the whole file. At stop time, we read all lines at once, parse them, and consume (delete) the file.

**Why assign to last entry**: All `post-tool-use` hooks between a `user-prompt-submit` and `stop` belong to the same prompt cycle. The last user-assistant pair in the IDE transcript is the active one, so all captured tool calls get assigned there.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`
